45	## 2. Retail data model + mock-data generators (external harness)
46	
47	> Domains → entities to generate. Believable retail data so checks/profiling/severity/trends look real.
48	
49	- [ ] **Product** — style, colour, material, SKU
50	- [ ] **Inventory** — on-hand stock, planned
51	- [ ] **Location** — store, address, store operating hours
52	- [ ] **Orders** — split **header** (order_number, channel, guest, status, order_total, tax, timestamps) ↔ **line items** (one row per SKU: qty, unit_price, line_total). Most checks run at line grain (`qty > 0`, `unit_price`, `line_total = qty × unit_price`); it's where `sku_id` joins Product; and `sum(line_total) = order_total` is an **intra-dataset reconciliation**
53	- [ ] **Sales lifecycle** — placed · shipped · audited events (each carries `order_number`)
54	- [ ] **Payments / Finance** — payment (method, status, captured amount), refund, settlement ledger — the reconciliation testbed: `order_total ↔ payment_captured ↔ refund` (feeds the reserved `comparison` kind, ADR 0014)
55	- [ ] **Return / Cancellation** — return, cancellation, guest, channel
56	- [ ] **Logistics** — shipment, tracking, carrier (+ fulfilling node)
57	- [ ] **Channel / Marketplace** — online · store · marketplace as a **first-class dimension** (a common DQ slice axis: null channel, orphan marketplace) — not just a `platform` column buried in returns
58	- [ ] **Guest / Customer** — guest details (name, address, **email, phone** — the PII targets for sample-row redaction #226), survey data, chatbot/feedback
59	- [ ] **Cross-cutting** — on-sale/discount, promotion/award/coupon, tax rate, exchange rate, product price, misc
60	- [ ] _Optional (domain realism, lower priority — add if a check needs them):_
61	  - **Supplier / Vendor + Purchase Orders** — inbound supply → inventory replenishment (freshness on inbound feeds)
62	  - **Fulfillment node / DC / warehouse** — the location that fulfilled an order (a join target beyond the store `Location`)
63	  - **Catalog hierarchy** — category · department · brand above Product (orphan / FK-integrity checks)
64	  - **Calendar / date dimension** — fiscal week · season for trend slicing
65	
66	**Route map — which dataset lands on which DataQ run path** (decided; per-flow detail in §3):
67	
68	| Dataset | Route | Platform · format / layer |
69	|---|---|---|
70	| Logistics **tracking events** | Flow C — flat-file in place | ADLS · **CSV** · batch-resolved (latest) |
71	| **Inventory daily snapshot** | Flow C — flat-file in place | ADLS · **Parquet** · latest-snapshot |
72	| **Orders** (header+line) · **Payments/Finance** · **Returns/Cancellations** · **Customer** master · **Product/SKU** master | Flow A — load to warehouse | **Snowflake** (SQL-heavy: custom-SQL, line-grain, reconciliation) |
73	| **Location** · **Promotions/pricing/coupons** · **Channel/Marketplace** | Flow A — load to warehouse | **Unity Catalog** (plain load — proves the UC runner *without* medallion) |
74	| **Feedback/survey/chatbot** · **Sales → revenue marts** | Flow B — medallion | **Databricks** bronze → silver → gold (UC) |
75	
76	> **Discipline — one system-of-record per entity.** Snowflake `Orders` is the truth; the medallion `gold` revenue is a *derived* aggregate that must reconcile **back** to it (Snowflake `order_total` ↔ Databricks `gold.daily_revenue` — the ADR 0014 `comparison` demo). Never author the same entity authoritatively in two places, or the reconciliation / FK checks measure generation noise instead of real DQ — the same guard as the key-consistency rule above.
77	>
78	> **Reserved-kind homes:** medallion **gold** tables host freshness/volume/anomaly monitors (ADR 0012 — daily aggregates that should refresh); the flat-file feeds host freshness + batch-resolution; the Snowflake↔Databricks pair hosts the cross-platform `comparison`.
79	
80	- [ ] Generator script (Python — Faker or similar) emitting CSV/Parquet to the ADLS landing zone
81	- [ ] **Keep shared keys consistent across domains AND across batch/incremental loads** — figure out a generation strategy (shared key pools / referential seeding) so the same identifier means the same entity everywhere:
82	  - `order_number` is identical across **Orders · Sales · Return · Logistics**
83	  - `sku_id` is identical across **Product · Inventory · Orders**
84	  - a shipment keeps **one `tracking_number`** across its `Ready For Shipment` → `In Transit` → `Delivered` events — and that number stays stable across different batches / data loads (events arrive incrementally)
85	  - _Why:_ without referential consistency the cross-dataset reconciliation / FK-orphan / join-based checks are meaningless (everything looks broken); the deliberate DQ issues below must be the **only** inconsistencies, not generation noise.
86	- [ ] **Shape ≥1 dataset for each reserved monitor kind** (ADR 0012 — "most real incidents are freshness/volume, not value-level; this is the leap from a GX runner to a DQ platform") so the seam has a live target when those kinds are built:
87	  - **Freshness · Volume · Anomaly** — make one feed **timestamped + incrementally loaded** (e.g. an hourly orders feed or a daily inventory snapshot with a `load_ts`): a freshness check ("last load < 1h"), a volume band ("rows within ±20%"), and an anomaly (sudden volume drop) each bite on it
88	  - **Schema drift** — one feed whose columns you can perturb across loads (add / drop / rename a column, change a type)
89	  - **Comparison / reconciliation** — the two source-vs-target pairs above (`line_total → order_total`; `order_total ↔ payment ↔ refund`) as the FDC-style diff target (ADR 0014; reserved now, built post-v1, but shape the data now)
90	  - _v1 only authors GX `expectation` checks — these kinds are **reserved** (ADR 0012); shaping the data now means the harness is ready the day they ship, no regen needed._
91	- [ ] **Seed some deliberate DQ issues** (nulls, dupes, FK orphans, stale dates, volume drops, a schema change, a reconciliation mismatch) so checks actually fail — proves severity tiers + alerting, not just green dashboards
92	- [ ] Parameterise volume + a `--seed` for reproducibility; document run command in harness README
93

96	## 3. Reference flows (each exercises a distinct DataQ run path)
97	
98	### 3a. Flow A — load to warehouse  →  `SnowflakeCheckRunner` / `UnityCatalogCheckRunner`
99	
100	> **Datasets:** **Snowflake** ← Orders (header+line) · Payments/Finance · Returns/Cancellations · Customer master · Product/SKU master (transactional + financial core; custom-SQL + line-grain + `sum(line_total)=order_total` + `order_total↔payment↔refund` reconciliation). **Unity Catalog (plain load)** ← Location · Promotions/pricing/coupons · Channel/Marketplace (governed reference data — proves the UC runner *without* the medallion machinery).
101	
102	- [ ] File lands in ADLS landing zone (from §2 generator)
103	- [ ] **ADF pipeline** (or Airflow DAG) picks the file up and loads it into Snowflake / UC
104	- [ ] On success, the orchestrator hits DataQ's trigger path (ADF webhook / Airflow callback → trigger binding)
105	- [ ] DataQ suite runs against the loaded **warehouse table**; result row persists
106	- [ ] Verify in `/results`: run succeeded, severity derived, metric_value populated
107	- [ ] **Snowflake suite** exercises a **custom-SQL** check (ADR 0019) — e.g. the `sum(line_total)=order_total` / `order_total↔payment↔refund` aggregate — not just column expectations
108	- [ ] **UC plain-load suite** runs on a Location / Promotions table (no Silver/Gold transform — that's Flow B)
109	
110	### 3b. Flow B — medallion  →  UC tables
111	
112	> **Datasets:** **Feedback/survey/chatbot** (bronze raw JSON/text → silver cleaned + **PII-handled** [#226] → gold sentiment/NPS feature table — the lakehouse/ML "feature store" story) and **Sales → revenue marts** (bronze raw sales events → silver cleaned daily sales by store·channel → gold revenue/units rollups).
113	
114	- [ ] File lands in **UC RAW (bronze)** layer
115	- [ ] **Databricks notebook** (external, not git-tracked) runs: bronze → silver → gold transforms
116	- [ ] DataQ suites run against the UC Silver/Gold tables
117	- [ ] **Gold tables host the reserved freshness/volume/anomaly targets** (ADR 0012 — daily aggregates that should refresh; "did the rollup load / did volume drop")
118	- [ ] **Cross-platform reconciliation target** (ADR 0014): Snowflake `Orders.order_total` ↔ Databricks `gold.daily_revenue` should tie out (the `comparison` demo; built post-v1, shaped now)
119	- [ ] (Optional) notebook completion triggers the DataQ run via Airflow/ADF binding
120	- [ ] Verify results per layer (bronze schema-drift, silver PII-redaction, gold freshness/volume)
121	
122	### 3c. Flow C — flat-file in place  →  `FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution
123	
124	> **Datasets:** **Logistics tracking events** (ADLS · **CSV** · event-append — `tracking_number` stable across `Ready For Shipment → In Transit → Delivered` batches) and **Inventory daily snapshot** (ADLS · **Parquet** · daily-snapshot — exercises the zero-copy Arrow read, PR #140). One of each format × each batch shape (event-append vs daily-snapshot).
125	
126	- [ ] File lands in ADLS and **stays a flat file** (no warehouse load)
127	- [ ] DataQ flat-file connection + suite targets the path with a **batching regex**
128	- [ ] Run resolves the **latest** batch and checks it directly
129	- [ ] Verify batch resolution picks the right file + result persists
130	- [ ] **CSV** path: tracking events — assert the tracking-number / event-status checks + freshness ("last drop < 1h")
131	- [ ] **Parquet** path: inventory snapshot — assert volume band (±20%) + freshness on the latest snapshot
132

149

151	
152	- [ ] Stand up Vault locally (dev mode / Docker) in the harness
153	- [ ] Prototype a `VaultSecretStore` against the existing `SecretStore` interface (spike branch, not merged)
154	- [ ] Confirm no interface change needed (if it needs one, that's a finding worth an ADR note)

158	
159	## 6. Performance / scale testing (post-v1 / backlog)
160	
161	> Rides §2's parameterizable volume (`--seed` + a `--volume` knob): generate **S / M / L / XL** of the same
162	> datasets, run, measure, record. **Baseline-first** — establish numbers + a regression budget; no hard SLAs
163	> yet (set them once we know where the knees are). Standalone harness script; the **QA/QE agent (§4)** can
164	> invoke it later as a perf-smoke. Needs §2 (generators) + §3 (the run paths) green first.
165	
166	**Lead axes (do these first):**
167	- [ ] **Run scaling** *(headline)* — GX run wall-clock vs **rows × #checks-per-suite**, per datasource (Snowflake · UC · flat-file CSV+Parquet). Capture throughput (rows/sec) + **worker memory peak** (GX pulls the batch in-memory) — and note whether time is in the warehouse query or GX itself.
168	- [ ] **Profiler latency** — profile a **wide** table (many columns) to quantify the known **N+1 per-column top-values** cost ([#327](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/327)) and the payoff once it's batched.
169	- [ ] **DB growth** — p95 latency of `/results`, `/dashboard/summary`, `/runs` as `results`/`runs` grow to 100k+ rows; validates the retention-sweep index ([#323](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/323)).
170	
171	**Secondary axes:**
172	- [ ] **Concurrency / throughput** — N suites at once: Celery pool saturation, the 60s scheduler beat under many due schedules (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), run-now storms → queue wait time.
173	- [ ] **Flat-file batch resolution at scale** — resolving the latest batch among a large landing zone (thousands of files) — the list+regex cost.
174	
175	**Method:**
176	- [ ] One script: param = datasource × volume tier × #checks; emits a CSV/JSON of (metric, value) per run
177	- [ ] Record a **baseline table** (commit it with the QA/QE agent in §4) + a **regression budget** (e.g. fail if a tier regresses > X% vs baseline) — so perf regressions are caught, not just measured once
178	- [ ] Capture both **app** metrics (run duration, profiler latency, API p95) and **resource** metrics (worker mem peak, DB CPU)
179	- [ ] Feed findings back: confirm/close #327 (profiler) + #323 (retention index); file new issues for any knee found
180

186	- [ ] When a flow first runs green end-to-end, update `docs/progress.md` (flip the ⬜ in the workstream table) and note the live smoke is discharged in CLAUDE.md §13

No credentials live in these files — every provider reads\n# its secrets from the environment exported by `source ../secrets.sh`:\n#   azurerm    -> ARM_SUBSCRIPTION_ID / ARM_TENANT_ID / ARM_CLIENT_ID / ARM_CLIENT_SECRET\n#   snowflake  -> SNOWFLAKE_USER / SNOWFLAKE_PASSWORD / SNOWFLAKE_ROLE\n#   databricks -> DATABRICKS_HOST / DATABRICKS_TOKEN\n# Only non-secret identifiers (org/account name) are passed as variables.\n\nprovider \"azurerm\" {\n  features {}\n  # subscription_id / tenant_id / client_id / client_secret come from ARM_* env vars.\n}\n\nprovider \"snowflake\" {\n  # user / password / role come from SNOWFLAKE_* env vars.\n  organization_name = var.snowflake_organization_name\n  account_name      = var.snowflake_account_name\n}\n\nprovider \"databricks\" {\n  # host / token come from DATABRICKS_HOST / DATABRICKS_TOKEN env vars.\n}\n\nprovider \"random\" {}\n"
}

2\"\n}\n\nvariable \"storage_account_prefix\" {\n  description = \"ADLS Gen2 account name prefix (a random suffix is appended for global uniqueness; 3-24 lowercase alphanumerics total).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"dataqharness\"\n}\n\nvariable \"adls_filesystems\" {\n  description = \"ADLS Gen2 filesystems (containers) for the landing zone.\"\n  type        = list(string)\n  default     = [\"landing\", \"raw\"]\n}\n\nvariable \"adf_factory_name\" {\n  description = \"Azure Data Factory instance name (Flow A load pipelines).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"dataq-harness-adf\"\n}\n\n# ---- Snowflake ------------------------------------------------------------\n# Account identifier PVQSOEQ-ZGB34383 = <organization>-<account_name>.\n\nvariable \"snowflake_organization_name\" {\n  description = \"Snowflake organization name (the part before the dash in the account locator).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"PVQSOEQ\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_account_name\" {\n  description = \"Snowflake account name (the part after the dash in the account locator).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"ZGB34383\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_database\" {\n  description = \"Database DataQ writes the warehouse-loaded retail datasets into (Flow A).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"DATAQ_DB\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_warehouse\" {\n  description = \"Virtual warehouse DataQ runs checks on.\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"DATAQ_WH\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_reader_role\" {\n  description = \"Least-privilege role DataQ uses to read (granted to the existing user, no new password).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"DATAQ_READER\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_reader_user\" {\n  description = \"Existing Snowflake user the reader role is granted to (DataQ connects as this user).\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"ROYARIJIT04\"\n}\n\nvariable \"snowflake_schemas\" {\n  description = \"Schemas created in the DataQ database (Flow A warehouse datasets).\"\n  type        = list(string)\n  default     = [\"RETAIL\"]\n}\n\n# ---- Databricks / Unity Catalog ------------------------------------------\n\nvariable \"uc_catalog\" {\n  description = \"Unity Catalog catalog for the harness retail data.\"\n  type        = string\n  default     = \"dataq_retail\"\n}\n\nvariable \"uc_schemas\" {\n  description = \"Medallion + reference schemas in the UC catalog.\"\n  type        = list(string)\n  default     = [\"raw\", \"silver\", \"gold\", \"reference\"]\n}\n"
}

{

  "content": "# Shared data sources + locals.\n\n# The RG already exists (§0); the SP has Contributor on it. Reference, don't create.\ndata \"azurerm_resource_group\" \"dataq\" {\n  name = var.azure_resource_group\n}\n\n# Random suffix to make the storage-account name globally unique.\nresource \"random_string\" \"suffix\" {\n  length  = 6\n  lower   = true\n  upper   = false\n  numeric = true\n  special = false\n}\n\nlocals {\n  common_tags = {\n    project = var.project_tag\n    managed = \"terraform\"\n    purpose = \"dataq-harness\"\n  }\n\n  # 3-24 lowercase alphanumerics; prefix + 6-char suffix.\n  storage_account_name = substr(\"${var.storage_account_prefix}${random_string.suffix.result}\", 0, 24)\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/adls.tf",
  "content": "# ADLS Gen2 — the flat-file landing zone (Flow C in place; Flow A/B source files).\n# HNS enabled = true makes this a Gen2 (hierarchical) account, not plain blob.\n\nresource \"azurerm_storage_account\" \"landing\" {\n  name                     = local.storage_account_name\n  resource_group_name      = data.azurerm_resource_group.dataq.name\n  location                 = var.azure_location\n  account_tier             = \"Standard\"\n  account_replication_type = \"LRS\"\n  account_kind             = \"StorageV2\"\n  is_hns_enabled           = true # Gen2 hierarchical namespace\n  min_tls_version          = \"TLS1_2\"\n\n  tags = local.common_tags\n}\n\nresource \"azurerm_storage_data_lake_gen2_filesystem\" \"zones\" {\n  for_each           = toset(var.adls_filesystems)\n  name               = each.value\n  storage_account_id = azurerm_storage_account.landing.id\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/snowflake.tf",
  "content": "# Snowflake — Flow A warehouse datasets (Orders/Payments/Returns/Customer/Product).\n# Creates the DB + schema(s) + warehouse + a least-privilege READER role, then grants\n# that role to the existing user (DataQ connects as that user — no new password/secret).\n\nresource \"snowflake_database\" \"dataq\" {\n  name    = var.snowflake_database\n  comment = \"DataQ harness retail datasets (Flow A).\"\n}\n\nresource \"snowflake_schema\" \"schemas\" {\n  for_each = toset(var.snowflake_schemas)\n  database = snowflake_database.dataq.name\n  name     = each.value\n  comment  = \"DataQ harness schema.\"\n}\n\nresource \"snowflake_warehouse\" \"dataq\" {\n  name                = var.snowflake_warehouse\n  warehouse_size      = \"XSMALL\"\n  auto_suspend        = 60   # seconds — keep the demo cheap\n  auto_resume         = true\n  initially_suspended = true\n  comment             = \"DataQ harness warehouse — checks run here.\"\n}\n\n# Least-privilege read role.\nresource \"snowflake_account_role\" \"reader\" {\n  name    = var.snowflake_reader_role\n  comment = \"DataQ read-only role: USAGE on db/schema/warehouse + SELECT on tables.\"\n}\n\n# USAGE on the warehouse.\nresource \"snowflake_grant_privileges_to_account_role\" \"wh_usage\" {\n  account_role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  privileges        = [\"USAGE\"]\n  on_account_object {\n    object_type = \"WAREHOUSE\"\n    object_name = snowflake_warehouse.dataq.name\n  }\n}\n\n# USAGE on the database.\nresource \"snowflake_grant_privileges_to_account_role\" \"db_usage\" {\n  account_role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  privileges        = [\"USAGE\"]\n  on_account_object {\n    object_type = \"DATABASE\"\n    object_name = snowflake_database.dataq.name\n  }\n}\n\n# USAGE + SELECT on each schema (current + future tables).\nresource \"snowflake_grant_privileges_to_account_role\" \"schema_usage\" {\n  for_each          = snowflake_schema.schemas\n  account_role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  privileges        = [\"USAGE\"]\n  on_schema {\n    schema_name = \"\\\"${snowflake_database.dataq.name}\\\".\\\"${each.value.name}\\\"\"\n  }\n}\n\nresource \"snowflake_grant_privileges_to_account_role\" \"table_select\" {\n  for_each          = snowflake_schema.schemas\n  account_role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  privileges        = [\"SELECT\"]\n  on_schema_object {\n    future {\n      object_type_plural = \"TABLES\"\n      in_schema          = \"\\\"${snowflake_database.dataq.name}\\\".\\\"${each.value.name}\\\"\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nresource \"snowflake_grant_privileges_to_account_role\" \"table_select_existing\" {\n  for_each          = snowflake_schema.schemas\n  account_role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  privileges        = [\"SELECT\"]\n  on_schema_object {\n    all {\n      object_type_plural = \"TABLES\"\n      in_schema          = \"\\\"${snowflake_database.dataq.name}\\\".\\\"${each.value.name}\\\"\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n# Grant the reader role to the existing user DataQ authenticates as.\nresource \"snowflake_grant_account_role\" \"reader_to_user\" {\n  role_name = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n  user_name = var.snowflake_reader_user\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/unity_catalog.tf",
  "content": "# Unity Catalog — Flow A (plain reference load) + Flow B (medallion bronze/silver/gold).\n# The workspace PAT's principal (workspace admin, §0) owns these; grants go to that user.\n\ndata \"databricks_current_user\" \"me\" {}\n\nresource \"databricks_catalog\" \"dataq\" {\n  name    = var.uc_catalog\n  comment = \"DataQ harness retail catalog (Flow A reference load + Flow B medallion).\"\n\n  properties = {\n    purpose = \"dataq-harness\"\n  }\n}\n\nresource \"databricks_schema\" \"schemas\" {\n  for_each     = toset(var.uc_schemas)\n  catalog_name = databricks_catalog.dataq.name\n  name         = each.value\n  comment      = \"DataQ harness ${each.value} layer.\"\n}\n\n# Catalog-level grants for the DataQ principal (the PAT user).\nresource \"databricks_grants\" \"catalog\" {\n  catalog = databricks_catalog.dataq.name\n  grant {\n    principal  = data.databricks_current_user.me.user_name\n    privileges = [\"USE_CATALOG\", \"USE_SCHEMA\", \"SELECT\"]\n  }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/outputs.tf",
  "content": "# Connection coordinates DataQ's connection editor needs. After `terraform apply`,\n# copy these into the harness env / secrets.sh blanks (SNOWFLAKE_*, ADF_FACTORY_NAME).\n# None of these are secrets (no passwords/keys/tokens are output).\n\noutput \"adls_storage_account\" {\n  description = \"ADLS Gen2 account name (DataQ flat-file connection host).\"\n  value       = azurerm_storage_account.landing.name\n}\n\noutput \"adls_dfs_endpoint\" {\n  description = \"ADLS Gen2 DFS endpoint.\"\n  value       = azurerm_storage_account.landing.primary_dfs_endpoint\n}\n\noutput \"adls_filesystems\" {\n  description = \"Landing-zone filesystems (containers).\"\n  value       = [for fs in azurerm_storage_data_lake_gen2_filesystem.zones : fs.name]\n}\n\noutput \"adf_factory_name\" {\n  description = \"ADF factory name -> fill ADF_FACTORY_NAME in secrets.sh.\"\n  value       = azurerm_data_factory.dataq.name\n}\n\noutput \"snowflake_database\" {\n  description = \"-> fill SNOWFLAKE_DATABASE in secrets.sh.\"\n  value       = snowflake_database.dataq.name\n}\n\noutput \"snowflake_warehouse\" {\n  description = \"-> fill SNOWFLAKE_WAREHOUSE in secrets.sh.\"\n  value       = snowflake_warehouse.dataq.name\n}\n\noutput \"snowflake_reader_role\" {\n  description = \"Least-privilege role -> switch SNOWFLAKE_ROLE in secrets.sh once tables are loaded.\"\n  value       = snowflake_account_role.reader.name\n}\n\noutput \"snowflake_schemas\" {\n  description = \"Schemas created in the DataQ database.\"\n  value       = [for s in snowflake_schema.schemas : s.name]\n}\n\noutput \"uc_catalog\" {\n  description = \"Unity Catalog catalog name (DataQ UC connection).\"\n  value       = databricks_catalog.dataq.name\n}\n\noutput \"uc_schemas\" {\n  description = \"UC schemas (medallion + reference).\"\n  value       = [for s in databricks_schema.schemas : s.name]\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/README.md",
  "content": "# DataQ harness — Terraform (§1 of HARNESS_TODO.md)\n\nProvisions the **datasources** for the live test/demo-data environment (ADR 0021):\nADLS Gen2 landing zone, Snowflake DB/warehouse/reader-role, Unity Catalog\ncatalog+schemas, and the ADF factory. Airflow is local Docker (§3), not provisioned here.\n\n**Secret-free by design:** every provider reads its credentials from the environment\nexported by `secrets.sh`. No passwords/keys/tokens live in `.tf` files or outputs.\n\n## Usage\n\n```bash\ncd ~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness\nsource secrets.sh                 # exports ARM_* / SNOWFLAKE_* / DATABRICKS_* \ncd terraform\nterraform init\nterraform validate\nterraform plan                    # review — creates nothing\nterraform apply                   # provisions real, billable resources\n```\n\nAfter `apply`, copy outputs back into `secrets.sh` blanks:\n`SNOWFLAKE_DATABASE`, `SNOWFLAKE_WAREHOUSE`, `ADF_FACTORY_NAME` (and switch\n`SNOWFLAKE_ROLE` to the reader role once Flow A has loaded tables).\n\n```bash\nterraform output                  # connection coordinates for DataQ's connection editor\n```\n\n## Teardown (the env is disposable)\n\n```bash\nterraform destroy\n```\n\n## What's where\n\n| File | Resources |\n|---|---|\n| `versions.tf`       | Terraform + provider pins, local state backend |\n| `providers.tf`      | Provider blocks (creds from env) |\n| `variables.tf`      | Non-secret config (regions, names) |\n| `main.tf`           | RG data source, random suffix, common tags |\n| `adls.tf`           | ADLS Gen2 account + `landing`/`raw` filesystems |\n| `snowflake.tf`      | DB + schema(s) + warehouse + least-priv reader role/grants |\n| `unity_catalog.tf`  | UC catalog + raw/silver/gold/reference schemas + grants |\n| `adf.tf`            | Data Factory instance (Flow A pipelines authored later) |\n| `outputs.tf`        | Connection coordinates (no secrets) |\n\n> **No Azure resource names leak into the DataQ repo** (ADR 0010/0013). They live only here.\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Two real findings from `plan` (exactly what the read-only check is for):

1. **azurerm** tries to auto-register ~60 resource providers at *subscription* scope — but the SP is Contributor on `dataq-rg` only → 403 spam. Fix: `resource_provider_registrations = "none"`.
2. **databricks** provider sees both the `ARM_*` and `DATABRICKS_*` env vars and can't decide → "more than one authorization method." Fix: pin `auth_type = "pat"`.

Both are provider-config fixes. Let me apply them.

## What I did — §1 Terraform skeleton (in `~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/terraform/`)

Authored a secret-free, apply-ready config (all creds read from `secrets.sh` env vars; nothing sensitive in `.tf` files or outputs):

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `versions.tf` | Provider pins + local (gitignored) state backend |
| `providers.tf` | azurerm / snowflake / databricks / random — creds from env |
| `variables.tf` | Non-secret config (regions, names) matching §0 |
| `main.tf` | RG data source, random suffix, common `project=dataq` tags |
| `adls.tf` | ADLS Gen2 account (HNS) + `landing`/`raw` filesystems |
| `snowflake.tf` | `DATAQ_DB` + `RETAIL` schema + `DATAQ_WH` + least-priv `DATAQ_READER` role (granted to your existing user — no new password) |
| `unity_catalog.tf` | `dataq_retail` catalog + raw/silver/gold/reference schemas + grants |
| `adf.tf` | `dataq-harness-adf` factory |
| `outputs.tf` | Connection coordinates for DataQ's connection editor |

Plus a `terraform/README.md` and a harness-root `.gitignore` (state holds secrets in plaintext).

**Verification:** `init` ✓ · `validate` ✓ · `fmt` ✓ · `plan` ✓ → **21 to add, 0 change, 0 destroy**, all three providers authenticate. The plan surfaced and I fixed two real provider-config issues: azurerm tried to register ~60 RPs at subscription scope (SP only has `dataq-rg`) → `resource_provider_registrations = "none"`; databricks saw both `ARM_*` and `DATABRICKS_*` and couldn't pick → pinned `auth_type = "pat"`. Tracker updated.

**Before you can `apply`:** a subscription **Owner** must pre-register `Microsoft.Storage` and `Microsoft.DataFactory` (the SP can't self-register; documented in the README). Then `source secrets.sh && terraform apply`, and copy outputs into the `secrets.sh` blanks (`SNOWFLAKE_DATABASE/WAREHOUSE`, `ADF_FACTORY_NAME`).

Want me to proceed to `apply` (I'd confirm the RPs are registered first), or move on to §2 (mock-data generators)?

## On your side question — the Entra user `dataq-admin@royarijit04outlook.onmicrosoft.com`

**Not needed for any of the Terraform/infra harness** — all provisioning runs as the service principal `dataq-terraform-sp` (`ARM_CLIENT_ID f6ab3a4d…`), not a user. So §1 doesn't touch it.

**But keep it — it's needed later for the app-level E2E.** DataQ's auth is Azure AD (MSAL) with real user login, and the workspace-admin surface is gated by the `WORKSPACE_ADMIN_EMAILS` allowlist. When you run Flows A/B/C and the QA/QE agent (§3/§4) against the actual DataQ app, you need a human identity to log in as and to be the workspace admin — `dataq-admin@…onmicrosoft.com` is the natural fit. Add its email to `WORKSPACE_ADMIN_EMAILS`.

One thing to confirm: it's only useful if it lives in the **same tenant** DataQ's MSAL app registration trusts. Your harness SP is in tenant `07b592a3-…`; the `…onmicrosoft.com` user may be in a different default tenant. If they don't match, either move the app registration or create the admin user in the SP's tenant. Want me to check which tenant the DataQ app registration targets?

**Confirmed — same tenant.** `royarijit04outlook.onmicrosoft.com` resolves to tenant `07b592a3-12ee-4631-abb1-8a7b73d91241`, exactly the tenant your harness SP lives in. So the `…onmicrosoft.com` suffix is just that tenant's default domain — not a separate directory.

So the Entra user is in the right place. To wire it up when you exercise the app E2E:
- Set `AZURE_TENANT_ID` / `VITE_AZURE_TENANT_ID` = `07b592a3-12ee-4631-abb1-8a7b73d91241` (the app is single-tenant; it'll then trust this user).
- Add `dataq-admin@royarijit04outlook.onmicrosoft.com` to `WORKSPACE_ADMIN_EMAILS` to make it the workspace admin.
- You'll still need to create the app registrations (`AZURE_API_CLIENT_ID` for the API, `AZURE_SPA_CLIENT_ID` for the SPA) in that tenant — those are separate from the Terraform SP, and that's app-setup (Week 7 / E2E), not part of §1.

**Net: keep the user — it's valid and correctly placed.** It's not used by the Terraform harness, but it's your app login + workspace-admin identity for the E2E flows.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/config.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Generation config: volume tiers + the §2 route map (dataset -> run path / format).\n\nNo secrets, no cloud calls — this module only describes *what* to generate and\n*where each dataset lands*. The emit layer (emit.py) writes to a local out/ dir\nmirroring the route map; uploading to ADLS / loading to Snowflake/UC is a later\nstep (§3, once Terraform §1 is applied).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Volume tiers — scale knobs for perf testing (§6 rides these via --tier).\n# Counts are \"base entities\"; transactional tables fan out from them.\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nVOLUME_TIERS: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {\n    \"S\": {\"customers\": 200, \"products\": 150, \"stores\": 8, \"promos\": 15, \"orders\": 800},\n    \"M\": {\"customers\": 1_000, \"products\": 600, \"stores\": 25, \"promos\": 40, \"orders\": 4_000},\n    \"L\": {\"customers\": 5_000, \"products\": 2_500, \"stores\": 80, \"promos\": 120, \"orders\": 20_000},\n    \"XL\": {\"customers\": 20_000, \"products\": 8_000, \"stores\": 250, \"promos\": 300, \"orders\": 100_000},\n}\n\nDEFAULT_TIER = \"S\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Route map (§2) — which dataset lands on which DataQ run path + file format.\n# emit.py uses `subdir` + `fmt` to lay out the output tree.\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Route:\n    \"\"\"Where a generated dataset belongs in the DataQ run-path topology.\"\"\"\n\n    flow: str  # \"A-snowflake\" | \"A-uc\" | \"B-medallion\" | \"C-flatfile\"\n    subdir: str  # path under out/ root\n    fmt: str  # \"csv\" | \"parquet\" | \"json\"\n\n\nROUTES: dict[str, Route] = {\n    # Flow A — load to warehouse (Snowflake): transactional + financial core.\n    \"products\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/products\", \"csv\"),\n    \"customers\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/customers\", \"csv\"),\n    \"orders_header\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/orders_header\", \"csv\"),\n    \"order_lines\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/order_lines\", \"csv\"),\n    \"payments\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/payments\", \"csv\"),\n    \"refunds\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/refunds\", \"csv\"),\n    \"returns\": Route(\"A-snowflake\", \"snowflake/returns\", \"csv\"),\n    # Flow A — Unity Catalog plain load: governed reference data (no medallion).\n    \"locations\": Route(\"A-uc\", \"unity_catalog/locations\", \"parquet\"),\n    \"channels\": Route(\"A-uc\", \"unity_catalog/channels\", \"parquet\"),\n    \"promotions\": Route(\"A-uc\", \"unity_catalog/promotions\", \"parquet\"),\n    # Flow B — medallion bronze source (Databricks notebook refines bronze->silver->gold).\n    \"sales_events\": Route(\"B-medallion\", \"databricks_medallion/bronze/sales_events\", \"json\"),\n    \"feedback\": Route(\"B-medallion\", \"databricks_medallion/bronze/feedback\", \"json\"),\n    # Flow C — flat-file in place (ADLS). Batch-resolved.\n    \"tracking_events\": Route(\"C-flatfile\", \"adls_flatfile/logistics_tracking\", \"csv\"),\n    \"inventory_snapshot\": Route(\"C-flatfile\", \"adls_flatfile/inventory_snapshot\", \"parquet\"),\n}\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass GenConfig:\n    \"\"\"Resolved generation parameters for one run.\"\"\"\n\n    tier: str = DEFAULT_TIER\n    seed: int = 42\n    out_dir: str = \"out\"\n    inject_issues: bool = True\n    issue_rate: float = 0.03  # fraction of rows touched by deliberate DQ issues\n    snapshots: int = 5  # inventory daily snapshots (exercises batch resolution + freshness)\n    tracking_batches: tuple[str, ...] = (\"ready\", \"in_transit\", \"delivered\")\n    counts: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def resolve(self) -> \"GenConfig\":\n        if self.tier not in VOLUME_TIERS:\n            raise ValueError(f\"unknown tier {self.tier!r}; choose from {list(VOLUME_TIERS)}\")\n        self.counts = dict(VOLUME_TIERS[self.tier])\n        return self\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/rng.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Seeded randomness — every value derives from one `random.Random(seed)` so a\ngiven (--tier, --seed) reproduces byte-for-byte. No third-party deps (no Faker):\nstdlib random + curated pools (pools.py) give tighter control over the referential\nconsistency §2 demands than Faker would.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport datetime as dt\nimport random\nfrom collections.abc import Sequence\nfrom typing import TypeVar\n\nT = TypeVar(\"T\")\n\n\nclass Rng:\n    \"\"\"Thin convenience wrapper over random.Random with id/date helpers.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, seed: int) -> None:\n        self._r = random.Random(seed)\n\n    # --- primitives -------------------------------------------------------\n    def choice(self, seq: Sequence[T]) -> T:\n        return self._r.choice(seq)\n\n    def choices(self, seq: Sequence[T], weights: Sequence[float], k: int = 1) -> list[T]:\n        return self._r.choices(seq, weights=weights, k=k)\n\n    def weighted(self, seq: Sequence[T], weights: Sequence[float]) -> T:\n        return self._r.choices(seq, weights=weights, k=1)[0]\n\n    def randint(self, lo: int, hi: int) -> int:\n        return self._r.randint(lo, hi)\n\n    def uniform(self, lo: float, hi: float) -> float:\n        return self._r.uniform(lo, hi)\n\n    def money(self, lo: float, hi: float) -> float:\n        return round(self._r.uniform(lo, hi), 2)\n\n    def chance(self, p: float) -> bool:\n        return self._r.random() < p\n\n    def sample(self, seq: Sequence[T], k: int) -> list[T]:\n        return self._r.sample(list(seq), k)\n\n    def shuffle(self, seq: list[T]) -> None:\n        self._r.shuffle(seq)\n\n    # --- ids --------------------------------------------------------------\n    def id_pool(self, prefix: str, n: int, width: int = 6) -> list[str]:\n        \"\"\"A stable, ordered pool of human-readable ids (e.g. SKU-000001).\"\"\"\n        return [f\"{prefix}-{i:0{width}d}\" for i in range(1, n + 1)]\n\n    # --- dates ------------------------------------------------------------\n    def datetime_between(self, start: dt.datetime, end: dt.datetime) -> dt.datetime:\n        span = int((end - start).total_seconds())\n        return start + dt.timedelta(seconds=self._r.randint(0, max(span, 1)))\n"
}

{

  "content": "\"\"\"Curated retail value pools. Believable, low-cardinality dimensions so checks /\nprofiling / severity look real. PII pools (names, email/phone shapes) are the\nredaction targets for sample-row handling (#226).\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nFIRST_NAMES = [\n    \"Aiden\", \"Maya\", \"Liam\", \"Sofia\", \"Noah\", \"Priya\", \"Ethan\", \"Zara\",\n    \"Lucas\", \"Amara\", \"Mason\", \"Nina\", \"Kai\", \"Leah\", \"Omar\", \"Iris\",\n    \"Ravi\", \"Elena\", \"Theo\", \"Yuki\",\n]\nLAST_NAMES = [\n    \"Patel\", \"Nguyen\", \"Garcia\", \"Khan\", \"Silva\", \"Chen\", \"Okafor\", \"Rossi\",\n    \"Kim\", \"Haddad\", \"Novak\", \"Tran\", \"Diaz\", \"Ahmed\", \"Mori\", \"Costa\",\n]\nSTREETS = [\"Maple Ave\", \"Oak St\", \"Cedar Ln\", \"Birch Rd\", \"Elm Way\", \"Pine Blvd\"]\nCITIES = [\n    (\"Seattle\", \"WA\"), (\"Portland\", \"OR\"), (\"Austin\", \"TX\"), (\"Denver\", \"CO\"),\n    (\"Chicago\", \"IL\"), (\"Boston\", \"MA\"), (\"Atlanta\", \"GA\"), (\"Phoenix\", \"AZ\"),\n]\n\nPRODUCT_CATEGORIES = [\"Apparel\", \"Footwear\", \"Accessories\", \"Home\", \"Beauty\"]\nCOLORS = [\"Black\", \"White\", \"Navy\", \"Crimson\", \"Olive\", \"Sand\", \"Slate\", \"Teal\"]\nMATERIALS = [\"Cotton\", \"Wool\", \"Leather\", \"Linen\", \"Polyester\", \"Denim\", \"Silk\"]\nSIZES = [\"XS\", \"S\", \"M\", \"L\", \"XL\"]\n\nCHANNELS = [\"online\", \"store\", \"marketplace\"]  # first-class dimension (§2)\nCHANNEL_WEIGHTS = [0.5, 0.35, 0.15]\n\nORDER_STATUSES = [\"placed\", \"shipped\", \"delivered\", \"returned\", \"cancelled\"]\nPAYMENT_METHODS = [\"card\", \"wallet\", \"gift_card\", \"bank_transfer\"]\nPAYMENT_STATUSES = [\"captured\", \"authorized\", \"failed\"]\nRETURN_REASONS = [\"wrong_size\", \"defective\", \"not_as_described\", \"changed_mind\", \"late\"]\nCARRIERS = [\"FleetEx\", \"PostNova\", \"RapidShip\", \"MetroCarry\"]\nTRACKING_STATUSES = [\"ready_for_shipment\", \"in_transit\", \"delivered\"]\n\nPROMO_TYPES = [\"percent_off\", \"amount_off\", \"bogo\", \"free_shipping\"]\nSURVEY_COMMENTS = [\n    \"Great fit and fast delivery.\",\n    \"Item arrived late but quality is good.\",\n    \"Color was different from the photo.\",\n    \"Excellent customer service.\",\n    \"Package was damaged in transit.\",\n    \"Would buy again.\",\n    \"Sizing runs small.\",\n    \"Loved it!\",\n]\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/generate.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Retail dataset generator with referential consistency (§2).\n\nOne system-of-record per entity. Shared key pools (sku_id, customer_id, store_id,\norder_number, tracking_number) mean the same id denotes the same entity everywhere —\nso the cross-dataset reconciliation / FK-orphan / join checks measure *real* DQ, not\ngeneration noise. The only inconsistencies are the deliberate ones injected by\ndq_issues.py.\n\nTimestamps are anchored to generation wall-clock `now` (offsets are seeded): the\n*latest* inventory snapshot / tracking-delivered batch are genuinely fresh, so the\nreserved freshness monitors (ADR 0012) bite. Everything else is reproducible per seed.\n\nReturns a uniform dict[str, DataFrame]. Multi-file datasets carry a split column:\n  - tracking_events: `batch`          (emit.py -> one CSV per batch)\n  - inventory_snapshot: `snapshot_date` (emit.py -> one Parquet per date)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport datetime as dt\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .config import GenConfig\nfrom .pools import (\n    CARRIERS,\n    CHANNEL_WEIGHTS,\n    CHANNELS,\n    CITIES,\n    COLORS,\n    FIRST_NAMES,\n    LAST_NAMES,\n    MATERIALS,\n    PAYMENT_METHODS,\n    PRODUCT_CATEGORIES,\n    PROMO_TYPES,\n    RETURN_REASONS,\n    SIZES,\n    STREETS,\n    SURVEY_COMMENTS,\n)\nfrom .rng import Rng\n\nTAX_RATE = 0.08\n\n\ndef generate(config: GenConfig) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:\n    rng = Rng(config.seed)\n    now = dt.datetime.now().replace(microsecond=0)\n    c = config.counts\n\n    products = _products(rng, c[\"products\"], now)\n    customers = _customers(rng, c[\"customers\"], now)\n    locations = _locations(rng, c[\"stores\"])\n    channels = _channels()\n    promotions = _promotions(rng, c[\"promos\"], now)\n\n    sku_ids = products[\"sku_id\"].tolist()\n    customer_ids = customers[\"customer_id\"].tolist()\n    store_ids = locations[\"store_id\"].tolist()\n    promo_ids = promotions[\"promo_id\"].tolist()\n\n    orders_header, order_lines = _orders(\n        rng, c[\"orders\"], now, sku_ids, customer_ids, store_ids, promo_ids, products\n    )\n    order_totals = dict(zip(orders_header[\"order_number\"], orders_header[\"order_total\"]))\n\n    payments = _payments(rng, orders_header, now)\n    refunds = _refunds(rng, orders_header, order_totals, now)\n    returns = _returns(rng, order_lines, now)\n\n    sales_events = _sales_events(orders_header)\n    feedback = _feedback(rng, customers, now)\n\n    tracking_events = _tracking_events(rng, orders_header, now, config.tracking_batches)\n    inventory_snapshot = _inventory_snapshot(rng, sku_ids, store_ids, now, config.snapshots)\n\n    return {\n        \"products\": products,\n        \"customers\": customers,\n        \"locations\": locations,\n        \"channels\": channels,\n        \"promotions\": promotions,\n        \"orders_header\": orders_header,\n        \"order_lines\": order_lines,\n        \"payments\": payments,\n        \"refunds\": refunds,\n        \"returns\": returns,\n        \"sales_events\": sales_events,\n        \"feedback\": feedback,\n        \"tracking_events\": tracking_events,\n        \"inventory_snapshot\": inventory_snapshot,\n    }\n\n\n# --- master data -----------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _products(rng: Rng, n: int, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    skus = rng.id_pool(\"SKU\", n)\n    rows = []\n    for sku in skus:\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"sku_id\": sku,\n                \"style_id\": f\"STY-{rng.randint(1000, 9999)}\",\n                \"category\": rng.choice(PRODUCT_CATEGORIES),\n                \"color\": rng.choice(COLORS),\n                \"material\": rng.choice(MATERIALS),\n                \"size\": rng.choice(SIZES),\n                \"unit_price\": rng.money(8.0, 240.0),\n                \"active\": rng.chance(0.92),\n                \"created_at\": rng.datetime_between(now - dt.timedelta(days=720), now),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _customers(rng: Rng, n: int, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    ids = rng.id_pool(\"CUST\", n)\n    rows = []\n    for cid in ids:\n        first = rng.choice(FIRST_NAMES)\n        last = rng.choice(LAST_NAMES)\n        city, state = rng.choice(CITIES)\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"customer_id\": cid,\n                \"first_name\": first,\n                \"last_name\": last,\n                \"email\": f\"{first}.{last}{rng.randint(1, 999)}@example.com\".lower(),  # PII (#226)\n                \"phone\": f\"+1-555-{rng.randint(100, 999)}-{rng.randint(1000, 9999)}\",  # PII\n                \"street\": f\"{rng.randint(100, 9999)} {rng.choice(STREETS)}\",\n                \"city\": city,\n                \"state\": state,\n                \"signup_date\": rng.datetime_between(now - dt.timedelta(days=1000), now).date(),\n                \"marketing_opt_in\": rng.chance(0.6),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _locations(rng: Rng, n: int) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    ids = rng.id_pool(\"STORE\", n, width=4)\n    rows = []\n    for sid in ids:\n        city, state = rng.choice(CITIES)\n        open_h = rng.randint(7, 10)\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"store_id\": sid,\n                \"store_name\": f\"{city} {rng.choice(['Center', 'Plaza', 'Market', 'Square'])}\",\n                \"street\": f\"{rng.randint(100, 9999)} {rng.choice(STREETS)}\",\n                \"city\": city,\n                \"state\": state,\n                \"open_hour\": open_h,\n                \"close_hour\": open_h + rng.randint(8, 12),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _channels() -> pd.DataFrame:\n    return pd.DataFrame(\n        {\n            \"channel\": CHANNELS,\n            \"description\": [\"Web storefront\", \"Physical retail\", \"Third-party marketplace\"],\n            \"is_online\": [True, False, True],\n        }\n    )\n\n\ndef _promotions(rng: Rng, n: int, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    ids = rng.id_pool(\"PROMO\", n, width=4)\n    rows = []\n    for pid in ids:\n        start = rng.datetime_between(now - dt.timedelta(days=180), now).date()\n        ptype = rng.choice(PROMO_TYPES)\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"promo_id\": pid,\n                \"promo_type\": ptype,\n                \"value\": (\n                    rng.randint(5, 40)\n                    if ptype == \"percent_off\"\n                    else rng.money(5.0, 50.0) if ptype == \"amount_off\" else 0\n                ),\n                \"channel\": rng.weighted(CHANNELS + [None], CHANNEL_WEIGHTS + [0.3]),\n                \"start_date\": start,\n                \"end_date\": start + dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(7, 60)),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\n# --- transactional core ----------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _orders(\n    rng: Rng,\n    n: int,\n    now: dt.datetime,\n    sku_ids: list[str],\n    customer_ids: list[str],\n    store_ids: list[str],\n    promo_ids: list[str],\n    products: pd.DataFrame,\n) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame]:\n    \"\"\"Header + line items. subtotal = sum(line_total); order_total = subtotal + tax.\n\n    This intra-dataset tie-out (sum(line_total) == order_total) is reconciliation\n    target #1 (ADR 0014) — shaped clean here; dq_issues.py breaks a few on purpose.\n    \"\"\"\n    price_by_sku = dict(zip(products[\"sku_id\"], products[\"unit_price\"]))\n    order_numbers = rng.id_pool(\"ORD\", n, width=8)\n\n    h: dict[str, list] = {k: [] for k in (\n        \"order_number\", \"customer_id\", \"channel\", \"store_id\", \"promo_id\",\n        \"order_ts\", \"status\", \"subtotal\", \"tax\", \"order_total\", \"currency\",\n    )}\n    line_rows: dict[str, list] = {k: [] for k in (\n        \"line_id\", \"order_number\", \"sku_id\", \"qty\", \"unit_price\", \"line_total\",\n    )}\n    line_seq = 0\n\n    for order_number in order_numbers:\n        channel = rng.weighted(CHANNELS, CHANNEL_WEIGHTS)\n        store_id = None if channel == \"online\" else rng.choice(store_ids)\n        n_lines = rng.randint(1, 5)\n        subtotal = 0.0\n        for _ in range(n_lines):\n            line_seq += 1\n            sku = rng.choice(sku_ids)\n            qty = rng.randint(1, 4)\n            unit_price = round(price_by_sku[sku], 2)\n            line_total = round(qty * unit_price, 2)\n            subtotal += line_total\n            line_rows[\"line_id\"].append(f\"OL-{line_seq:09d}\")\n            line_rows[\"order_number\"].append(order_number)\n            line_rows[\"sku_id\"].append(sku)\n            line_rows[\"qty\"].append(qty)\n            line_rows[\"unit_price\"].append(unit_price)\n            line_rows[\"line_total\"].append(line_total)\n\n        subtotal = round(subtotal, 2)\n        tax = round(subtotal * TAX_RATE, 2)\n        order_total = round(subtotal + tax, 2)\n        h[\"order_number\"].append(order_number)\n        h[\"customer_id\"].append(rng.choice(customer_ids))\n        h[\"channel\"].append(channel)\n        h[\"store_id\"].append(store_id)\n        h[\"promo_id\"].append(rng.choice(promo_ids) if rng.chance(0.3) else None)\n        h[\"order_ts\"].append(rng.datetime_between(now - dt.timedelta(days=120), now))\n        h[\"status\"].append(\n            rng.weighted(\n                [\"placed\", \"shipped\", \"delivered\", \"returned\", \"cancelled\"],\n                [0.15, 0.2, 0.5, 0.1, 0.05],\n            )\n        )\n        h[\"subtotal\"].append(subtotal)\n        h[\"tax\"].append(tax)\n        h[\"order_total\"].append(order_total)\n        h[\"currency\"].append(\"USD\")\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(h), pd.DataFrame(line_rows)\n\n\ndef _payments(rng: Rng, orders: pd.DataFrame, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"captured_amount == order_total when captured — reconciliation target #2.\"\"\"\n    rows = []\n    for _, o in orders.iterrows():\n        status = rng.weighted([\"captured\", \"authorized\", \"failed\"], [0.9, 0.06, 0.04])\n        captured = float(o[\"order_total\"]) if status == \"captured\" else 0.0\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"payment_id\": f\"PAY-{o['order_number'][4:]}\",\n                \"order_number\": o[\"order_number\"],\n                \"method\": rng.choice(PAYMENT_METHODS),\n                \"status\": status,\n                \"captured_amount\": round(captured, 2),\n                \"paid_ts\": o[\"order_ts\"] + dt.timedelta(minutes=rng.randint(1, 90)),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _refunds(\n    rng: Rng, orders: pd.DataFrame, order_totals: dict[str, float], now: dt.datetime\n) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    rows = []\n    refundable = orders[orders[\"status\"].isin([\"returned\", \"cancelled\"])]\n    for _, o in refundable.iterrows():\n        total = order_totals[o[\"order_number\"]]\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"refund_id\": f\"REF-{o['order_number'][4:]}\",\n                \"order_number\": o[\"order_number\"],\n                \"refund_amount\": round(total * rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0), 2),\n                \"reason\": rng.choice(RETURN_REASONS),\n                \"refund_ts\": o[\"order_ts\"] + dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(1, 21)),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _returns(rng: Rng, lines: pd.DataFrame, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    sample = lines.sample(frac=0.08, random_state=7) if len(lines) else lines\n    rows = []\n    for i, ln in enumerate(sample.itertuples(index=False), start=1):\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"return_id\": f\"RET-{i:08d}\",\n                \"order_number\": ln.order_number,\n                \"sku_id\": ln.sku_id,\n                \"qty\": min(ln.qty, rng.randint(1, 2)),\n                \"reason\": rng.choice(RETURN_REASONS),\n                \"channel\": rng.weighted(CHANNELS, CHANNEL_WEIGHTS),\n                \"return_ts\": now - dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(0, 60)),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\n# --- Flow B medallion bronze sources ---------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _sales_events(orders: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"One row per (order, lifecycle event). gold.daily_revenue rolls these up and\n    must reconcile back to Snowflake order_total (cross-platform comparison, ADR 0014).\"\"\"\n    rows = []\n    for _, o in orders.iterrows():\n        base_ts = o[\"order_ts\"]\n        for k, (etype, offset_h) in enumerate(\n            [(\"placed\", 0), (\"shipped\", 24), (\"audited\", 72)]\n        ):\n            rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"event_id\": f\"EVT-{o['order_number'][4:]}-{k}\",\n                    \"order_number\": o[\"order_number\"],\n                    \"event_type\": etype,\n                    \"event_ts\": base_ts + dt.timedelta(hours=offset_h),\n                    \"channel\": o[\"channel\"],\n                    \"store_id\": o[\"store_id\"],\n                    \"amount\": float(o[\"order_total\"]) if etype == \"placed\" else None,\n                }\n            )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _feedback(rng: Rng, customers: pd.DataFrame, now: dt.datetime) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    sample = customers.sample(frac=0.4, random_state=11) if len(customers) else customers\n    rows = []\n    for i, cust in enumerate(sample.itertuples(index=False), start=1):\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"feedback_id\": f\"FB-{i:08d}\",\n                \"customer_id\": cust.customer_id,\n                \"channel\": rng.weighted(CHANNELS, CHANNEL_WEIGHTS),\n                \"rating\": rng.randint(1, 5),\n                \"comment\": rng.choice(SURVEY_COMMENTS),\n                \"email\": cust.email,  # PII — silver layer redacts (#226)\n                \"created_ts\": now - dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(0, 90)),\n            }\n        )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\n# --- Flow C flat files -----------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _tracking_events(\n    rng: Rng, orders: pd.DataFrame, now: dt.datetime, batches: tuple[str, ...]\n) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"A shipment keeps ONE tracking_number across ready->in_transit->delivered events\n    that arrive in separate batches (§2). emit.py splits by `batch` into CSV files.\"\"\"\n    shipped = orders[orders[\"status\"].isin([\"shipped\", \"delivered\", \"returned\"])]\n    status_for_batch = {\n        \"ready\": \"ready_for_shipment\",\n        \"in_transit\": \"in_transit\",\n        \"delivered\": \"delivered\",\n    }\n    # batch recency: ready oldest, delivered newest (so freshness on delivered passes).\n    batch_age_days = {\"ready\": 5, \"in_transit\": 2, \"delivered\": 0}\n    rows = []\n    for _, o in shipped.iterrows():\n        tracking_number = f\"TRK{o['order_number'][4:]}\"\n        carrier = rng.choice(CARRIERS)\n        city, _state = rng.choice(CITIES)\n        for batch in batches:\n            rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"tracking_number\": tracking_number,\n                    \"order_number\": o[\"order_number\"],\n                    \"carrier\": carrier,\n                    \"status\": status_for_batch[batch],\n                    \"event_ts\": now - dt.timedelta(days=batch_age_days[batch], hours=rng.randint(0, 12)),\n                    \"location_city\": city,\n                    \"batch\": batch,\n                }\n            )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n\ndef _inventory_snapshot(\n    rng: Rng, sku_ids: list[str], store_ids: list[str], now: dt.datetime, snapshots: int\n) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Daily snapshot over a stable sku x store grid. emit.py splits by `snapshot_date`\n    into Parquet (zero-copy Arrow read, PR #140). The latest snapshot is fresh (load_ts\n    ~now) so freshness passes; volume band / anomaly ride the per-day row counts.\"\"\"\n    # Keep the grid bounded for large tiers — sample sku/store pairs once, reuse daily.\n    grid_skus = sku_ids if len(sku_ids) <= 400 else rng.sample(sku_ids, 400)\n    grid_stores = store_ids if len(store_ids) <= 20 else rng.sample(store_ids, 20)\n    pairs = [(s, st) for s in grid_skus for st in grid_stores]\n\n    rows = []\n    for d in range(snapshots):\n        snap_date = (now - dt.timedelta(days=snapshots - 1 - d)).date()\n        load_ts = now - dt.timedelta(days=snapshots - 1 - d, hours=rng.randint(0, 3))\n        for sku, store in pairs:\n            rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"sku_id\": sku,\n                    \"store_id\": store,\n                    \"on_hand\": rng.randint(0, 500),\n                    \"planned\": rng.randint(0, 200),\n                    \"snapshot_date\": snap_date,\n                    \"load_ts\": load_ts,\n                }\n            )\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n"
}

Pandas typing noise only. Now the deliberate-DQ-issue injector — this is what turns "checks should fail" into "checks fail *exactly here*" (emits a manifest the QA/QE agent can assert against):

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01RscDzHHY6sEtascTm2CXRX
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/dq_issues.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Deliberate DQ-issue injection (§2: \"seed some deliberate DQ issues … so checks\nactually fail — proves severity tiers + alerting, not just green dashboards\").\n\nEvery injected issue is recorded in a manifest (written by emit.py as\n`_dq_issues_manifest.json`) so the QA/QE agent (§4) can assert the checks fail\n*exactly* where we broke the data — and nowhere else (that's why referential\nconsistency in generate.py matters: no accidental failures to confuse the signal).\n\nRow-level issues are applied here (mutating the in-memory frames). The cross-file\nschema-drift issue is applied in emit.py (it perturbs one snapshot file's columns)\nand appended to the same manifest.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport datetime as dt\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .config import GenConfig\n\n\ndef inject(datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], config: GenConfig) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Mutate `datasets` in place; return the list of injected-issue records.\"\"\"\n    issues: list[dict] = []\n    rate = config.issue_rate\n\n    def record(kind: str, dataset: str, detail: str, keys: list) -> None:\n        issues.append(\n            {\"kind\": kind, \"dataset\": dataset, \"detail\": detail, \"n\": len(keys), \"keys\": keys[:50]}\n        )\n\n    # 1. Nulls — required dimension / PII columns.\n    orders = datasets[\"orders_header\"]\n    idx = _sample_idx(orders, rate, seed=101)\n    orders.loc[idx, \"channel\"] = None\n    record(\"null\", \"orders_header\", \"channel set NULL (required slice dimension)\", orders.loc[idx, \"order_number\"].tolist())\n\n    customers = datasets[\"customers\"]\n    idx = _sample_idx(customers, rate, seed=102)\n    customers.loc[idx, \"email\"] = None\n    record(\"null\", \"customers\", \"email set NULL (PII / contactability)\", customers.loc[idx, \"customer_id\"].tolist())\n\n    # 2. Duplicates — exact-row dupes break uniqueness checks.\n    lines = datasets[\"order_lines\"]\n    dup = lines.sample(frac=rate, random_state=103)\n    datasets[\"order_lines\"] = pd.concat([lines, dup], ignore_index=True)\n    record(\"duplicate\", \"order_lines\", \"exact-row duplicates appended\", dup[\"line_id\"].tolist())\n\n    products = datasets[\"products\"]\n    dup_sku = products.sample(n=max(1, int(len(products) * rate * 0.5)), random_state=104)\n    datasets[\"products\"] = pd.concat([products, dup_sku], ignore_index=True)\n    record(\"duplicate\", \"products\", \"duplicate sku_id rows (PK violation)\", dup_sku[\"sku_id\"].tolist())\n\n    # 3. FK orphans — point a few children at non-existent parents.\n    lines = datasets[\"order_lines\"]\n    idx = _sample_idx(lines, rate * 0.5, seed=105)\n    lines.loc[idx, \"sku_id\"] = \"SKU-000000\"  # never generated\n    record(\"fk_orphan\", \"order_lines\", \"sku_id -> non-existent product SKU-000000\", lines.loc[idx, \"line_id\"].tolist())\n\n    idx = _sample_idx(orders, rate * 0.5, seed=106)\n    orders.loc[idx, \"customer_id\"] = \"CUST-000000\"  # never generated\n    record(\"fk_orphan\", \"orders_header\", \"customer_id -> non-existent CUST-000000\", orders.loc[idx, \"order_number\"].tolist())\n\n    # 4. Reconciliation mismatch #1 — sum(line_total)+tax != order_total (ADR 0014).\n    idx = _sample_idx(orders, rate * 0.5, seed=107)\n    orders.loc[idx, \"order_total\"] = (orders.loc[idx, \"order_total\"] * 1.15).round(2)\n    record(\"reconciliation\", \"orders_header\", \"order_total inflated vs sum(line_total)+tax\", orders.loc[idx, \"order_number\"].tolist())\n\n    # 5. Reconciliation mismatch #2 — captured_amount != order_total.\n    payments = datasets[\"payments\"]\n    captured = payments[payments[\"status\"] == \"captured\"]\n    if len(captured):\n        idx = _sample_idx(captured, rate * 0.5, seed=108)\n        payments.loc[idx, \"captured_amount\"] = (payments.loc[idx, \"captured_amount\"] - 7.77).round(2)\n        record(\"reconciliation\", \"payments\", \"captured_amount != order_total (underpayment)\", payments.loc[idx, \"payment_id\"].tolist())\n\n    # 6. Stale freshness — push the LATEST inventory snapshot's load_ts ~26h back so a\n    #    \"last load < 1h/24h\" freshness monitor FAILS on the newest batch.\n    inv = datasets[\"inventory_snapshot\"]\n    latest_date = inv[\"snapshot_date\"].max()\n    latest_mask = inv[\"snapshot_date\"] == latest_date\n    inv.loc[latest_mask, \"load_ts\"] = inv.loc[latest_mask, \"load_ts\"] - dt.timedelta(hours=26)\n    record(\"stale\", \"inventory_snapshot\", f\"latest snapshot {latest_date} load_ts pushed 26h back\", [str(latest_date)])\n\n    # 7. Volume drop — gut a mid snapshot's row count for a volume-band / anomaly hit.\n    dates = sorted(inv[\"snapshot_date\"].unique())\n    if len(dates) >= 3:\n        drop_date = dates[len(dates) // 2]\n        drop_mask = inv[\"snapshot_date\"] == drop_date\n        keep = inv[~drop_mask]\n        thinned = inv[drop_mask].sample(frac=0.3, random_state=109)\n        datasets[\"inventory_snapshot\"] = pd.concat([keep, thinned], ignore_index=True)\n        record(\"volume_drop\", \"inventory_snapshot\", f\"snapshot {drop_date} thinned to ~30% rows\", [str(drop_date)])\n\n    return issues\n\n\ndef _sample_idx(df: pd.DataFrame, frac: float, seed: int) -> pd.Index:\n    n = max(1, int(len(df) * frac))\n    return df.sample(n=min(n, len(df)), random_state=seed).index\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/emit.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Write generated datasets to a local out/ tree mirroring the §2 route map.\n\nLayout (out/ root):\n  snowflake/<table>/<table>.csv                 Flow A -> Snowflake (COPY INTO source)\n  unity_catalog/<table>/<table>.parquet         Flow A -> UC plain load\n  databricks_medallion/bronze/<src>/<src>.json  Flow B -> medallion bronze (NDJSON)\n  adls_flatfile/logistics_tracking/tracking_events_<batch>.csv   Flow C (event-append)\n  adls_flatfile/inventory_snapshot/inventory_<date>.parquet      Flow C (daily snapshot)\n\nTwo split datasets become multiple files:\n  tracking_events  -> one CSV per `batch`        (same tracking_number recurs across files)\n  inventory_snapshot -> one Parquet per `snapshot_date`\n\nSchema-drift (the one cross-file deliberate issue) is applied here: the newest\ninventory snapshot file renames on_hand -> qty_on_hand, appended to the DQ manifest.\n\nWrites two sidecar manifests at the out/ root:\n  _manifest.json            dataset -> {flow, format, files, rows}\n  _dq_issues_manifest.json  the deliberate failures (row-level from dq_issues + schema drift)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .config import ROUTES, GenConfig\n\n\ndef write(datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], config: GenConfig, dq_issues: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    root = Path(config.out_dir)\n    root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    file_manifest: dict[str, dict] = {}\n\n    for name, df in datasets.items():\n        route = ROUTES[name]\n        target = root / route.subdir\n        target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        if name == \"tracking_events\":\n            files = _write_tracking(df, target)\n        elif name == \"inventory_snapshot\":\n            files, drift = _write_inventory(df, target, config.inject_issues)\n            if drift:\n                dq_issues.append(drift)\n        else:\n            files = [_write_single(df, target, name, route.fmt)]\n\n        file_manifest[name] = {\n            \"flow\": route.flow,\n            \"format\": route.fmt,\n            \"rows\": int(len(df)),\n            \"files\": [str(p.relative_to(root)) for p in files],\n        }\n\n    (root / \"_manifest.json\").write_text(json.dumps(file_manifest, indent=2, default=str))\n    (root / \"_dq_issues_manifest.json\").write_text(json.dumps(dq_issues, indent=2, default=str))\n    return file_manifest\n\n\ndef _write_single(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> Path:\n    path = target / f\"{name}.{fmt}\"\n    if fmt == \"csv\":\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"parquet\":\n        df.to_parquet(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"json\":  # NDJSON bronze raw\n        df.to_json(path, orient=\"records\", lines=True, date_format=\"iso\")\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown format {fmt!r}\")\n    return path\n\n\ndef _write_tracking(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    paths = []\n    for batch, group in df.groupby(\"batch\"):\n        path = target / f\"tracking_events_{batch}.csv\"\n        group.drop(columns=[\"batch\"]).to_csv(path, index=False)\n        paths.append(path)\n    return paths\n\n\ndef _write_inventory(\n    df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, inject_drift: bool\n) -> tuple[list[Path], dict | None]:\n    paths = []\n    drift: dict | None = None\n    latest_date = df[\"snapshot_date\"].max()\n    for snap_date, group in df.groupby(\"snapshot_date\"):\n        out = group.drop(columns=[\"snapshot_date\"])\n        if inject_drift and snap_date == latest_date:\n            out = out.rename(columns={\"on_hand\": \"qty_on_hand\"})  # schema drift on newest load\n            drift = {\n                \"kind\": \"schema_drift\",\n                \"dataset\": \"inventory_snapshot\",\n                \"detail\": f\"newest snapshot {snap_date} renames on_hand -> qty_on_hand\",\n                \"n\": 1,\n                \"keys\": [str(snap_date)],\n            }\n        path = target / f\"inventory_{snap_date}.parquet\"\n        out.to_parquet(path, index=False)\n        paths.append(path)\n    return paths, drift\n"
}

Dependency-light: pandas + pyarrow + stdlib only.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom .config import DEFAULT_TIER, VOLUME_TIERS, GenConfig\nfrom .generate import generate\n\n__all__ = [\"GenConfig\", \"generate\", \"VOLUME_TIERS\", \"DEFAULT_TIER\"]\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/__main__.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CLI: python -m mockdata --tier S --seed 42 --out ./out\n\nReproducible per (--tier, --seed) except wall-clock-anchored timestamps (so the\nlatest inventory snapshot / tracking-delivered batch are genuinely fresh — see\ngenerate.py). Use --no-issues to emit clean data (all checks green).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\n\nfrom .config import DEFAULT_TIER, VOLUME_TIERS, GenConfig\nfrom .dq_issues import inject\nfrom .emit import write\nfrom .generate import generate\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"mockdata\", description=__doc__)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--tier\", choices=list(VOLUME_TIERS), default=DEFAULT_TIER)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--seed\", type=int, default=42)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--out\", default=\"out\", help=\"output directory root\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--snapshots\", type=int, default=5, help=\"inventory daily snapshots\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--issue-rate\", type=float, default=0.03)\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--no-issues\",\n        dest=\"inject_issues\",\n        action=\"store_false\",\n        help=\"emit clean data (no deliberate DQ issues)\",\n    )\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    config = GenConfig(\n        tier=args.tier,\n        seed=args.seed,\n        out_dir=args.out,\n        inject_issues=args.inject_issues,\n        issue_rate=args.issue_rate,\n        snapshots=args.snapshots,\n    ).resolve()\n\n    print(f\"generating tier={config.tier} seed={config.seed} -> {config.out_dir}/\")\n    datasets = generate(config)\n    issues = inject(datasets, config) if config.inject_issues else []\n    manifest = write(datasets, config, issues)\n\n    total_rows = sum(m[\"rows\"] for m in manifest.values())\n    print(f\"  {len(manifest)} datasets, {total_rows:,} rows\")\n    for name, m in manifest.items():\n        print(f\"    {name:<20} {m['rows']:>8,} rows  [{m['flow']}]  {len(m['files'])} file(s)\")\n    if issues:\n        print(f\"  {len(issues)} deliberate DQ issues -> {config.out_dir}/_dq_issues_manifest.json\")\n        for it in issues:\n            print(f\"    {it['kind']:<14} {it['dataset']:<20} n={it['n']:<5} {it['detail']}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/README.md",
  "content": "# DataQ harness — mock-data generator (§2 of HARNESS_TODO.md)\n\nBelievable, **referentially-consistent** retail data across the four DataQ run\npaths, with **deliberate DQ issues seeded and manifested** so checks / severity /\nalerting have something real to bite on (not just green dashboards).\n\nDependency-light: **pandas + pyarrow + stdlib only** (no Faker — stdlib `random`\n+ curated pools give tighter control over the shared-key consistency §2 needs).\n\n## Run\n\n```bash\nconda activate dataq            # has pandas + pyarrow\ncd ~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness\npython -m mockdata --tier S --seed 42 --out ./out\n```\n\n| Flag | Default | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `--tier` | `S` | volume tier `S` / `M` / `L` / `XL` (perf §6 rides this) |\n| `--seed` | `42` | reproducible per seed (except wall-clock timestamps, see below) |\n| `--out` | `out` | output directory root |\n| `--snapshots` | `5` | inventory daily snapshots (batch resolution + freshness) |\n| `--issue-rate` | `0.03` | fraction of rows touched by deliberate issues |\n| `--no-issues` | — | emit **clean** data (all checks green) |\n\n> **Timestamps are anchored to generation wall-clock `now`** (offsets are seeded):\n> the *latest* inventory snapshot and the tracking `delivered` batch are genuinely\n> fresh, so the reserved **freshness** monitors (ADR 0012) bite. Everything else is\n> reproducible per `--seed`.\n\n## What it emits (the §2 route map)\n\n| Dataset(s) | Flow / run path | Format |\n|---|---|---|\n| products, customers, orders_header, order_lines, payments, refunds, returns | **A — Snowflake** (custom-SQL, line-grain, reconciliation) | CSV |\n| locations, channels, promotions | **A — Unity Catalog** plain load (reference data) | Parquet |\n| sales_events, feedback | **B — Databricks medallion** bronze source | NDJSON |\n| tracking_events (event-append) | **C — flat-file** in place (ADLS) | CSV per batch |\n| inventory_snapshot (daily) | **C — flat-file** in place (ADLS) | Parquet per date |\n\nOutput tree mirrors the route map (`out/snowflake/…`, `out/unity_catalog/…`,\n`out/databricks_medallion/bronze/…`, `out/adls_flatfile/…`). Two sidecar manifests\nland at the `out/` root:\n\n- `_manifest.json` — dataset → `{flow, format, files, rows}`\n- `_dq_issues_manifest.json` — the deliberate failures, each with the affected `keys`\n\n## Referential consistency (the hard part)\n\nOne system-of-record per entity; shared key pools mean the same id denotes the same\nentity everywhere:\n\n- `order_number` ties Orders ↔ Payments ↔ Refunds ↔ Returns ↔ Sales events ↔ Tracking\n- `sku_id` ties Products ↔ Order lines ↔ Inventory ↔ Returns\n- one `tracking_number` is stable across `ready → in_transit → delivered` batch files\n\nSo the cross-dataset reconciliation / FK-orphan / join checks measure **real** DQ —\nthe only inconsistencies are the deliberate ones.\n\n## Reserved-monitor-kind shaping (ADR 0012/0014 — built post-v1, shaped now)\n\n- **Freshness / volume / anomaly** → `inventory_snapshot` (timestamped `load_ts`,\n  daily batches; one snapshot is thinned for a volume-band/anomaly hit)\n- **Schema drift** → newest `inventory_snapshot` renames `on_hand → qty_on_hand`\n- **Comparison / reconciliation** → in-dataset `sum(line_total)+tax == order_total`;\n  cross-table `order_total ↔ payment.captured ↔ refund`; cross-platform\n  Snowflake `order_total` ↔ Databricks `gold.daily_revenue` (sales_events roll-up)\n\n## Deliberate DQ issues (default on)\n\nnulls (channel, email) · duplicates (order_lines, product sku) · FK orphans\n(`SKU-000000`, `CUST-000000`) · reconciliation mismatch (order_total, payment) ·\nstale freshness (latest snapshot `load_ts` +26h back) · volume drop (mid snapshot\nthinned to ~30%) · schema drift (newest snapshot). All recorded in\n`_dq_issues_manifest.json` with affected keys.\n\n## Next (§3)\n\n`out/` is local. Uploading to the ADLS landing zone + COPY INTO Snowflake / load to\nUC happens in §3 once Terraform (§1) is applied. The folder layout is already the\nlanding-zone shape.\n"
}

45	## 2. Retail data model + mock-data generators (external harness)
46	
47	> Domains → entities to generate. Believable retail data so checks/profiling/severity/trends look real.
48	
49	- [ ] **Product** — style, colour, material, SKU
50	- [ ] **Inventory** — on-hand stock, planned
51	- [ ] **Location** — store, address, store operating hours
52	- [ ] **Orders** — split **header** (order_number, channel, guest, status, order_total, tax, timestamps) ↔ **line items** (one row per SKU: qty, unit_price, line_total). Most checks run at line grain (`qty > 0`, `unit_price`, `line_total = qty × unit_price`); it's where `sku_id` joins Product; and `sum(line_total) = order_total` is an **intra-dataset reconciliation**
53	- [ ] **Sales lifecycle** — placed · shipped · audited events (each carries `order_number`)
54	- [ ] **Payments / Finance** — payment (method, status, captured amount), refund, settlement ledger — the reconciliation testbed: `order_total ↔ payment_captured ↔ refund` (feeds the reserved `comparison` kind, ADR 0014)
55	- [ ] **Return / Cancellation** — return, cancellation, guest, channel
56	- [ ] **Logistics** — shipment, tracking, carrier (+ fulfilling node)
57	- [ ] **Channel / Marketplace** — online · store · marketplace as a **first-class dimension** (a common DQ slice axis: null channel, orphan marketplace) — not just a `platform` column buried in returns
58	- [ ] **Guest / Customer** — guest details (name, address, **email, phone** — the PII targets for sample-row redaction #226), survey data, chatbot/feedback
59	- [ ] **Cross-cutting** — on-sale/discount, promotion/award/coupon, tax rate, exchange rate, product price, misc
60	- [ ] _Optional (domain realism, lower priority — add if a check needs them):_
61	  - **Supplier / Vendor + Purchase Orders** — inbound supply → inventory replenishment (freshness on inbound feeds)
62	  - **Fulfillment node / DC / warehouse** — the location that fulfilled an order (a join target beyond the store `Location`)
63	  - **Catalog hierarchy** — category · department · brand above Product (orphan / FK-integrity checks)
64	  - **Calendar / date dimension** — fiscal week · season for trend slicing
65	
66	**Route map — which dataset lands on which DataQ run path** (decided; per-flow detail in §3):
67	
68	| Dataset | Route | Platform · format / layer |
69	|---|---|---|
70	| Logistics **tracking events** | Flow C — flat-file in place | ADLS · **CSV** · batch-resolved (latest) |
71	| **Inventory daily snapshot** | Flow C — flat-file in place | ADLS · **Parquet** · latest-snapshot |
72	| **Orders** (header+line) · **Payments/Finance** · **Returns/Cancellations** · **Customer** master · **Product/SKU** master | Flow A — load to warehouse | **Snowflake** (SQL-heavy: custom-SQL, line-grain, reconciliation) |
73	| **Location** · **Promotions/pricing/coupons** · **Channel/Marketplace** | Flow A — load to warehouse | **Unity Catalog** (plain load — proves the UC runner *without* medallion) |
74	| **Feedback/survey/chatbot** · **Sales → revenue marts** | Flow B — medallion | **Databricks** bronze → silver → gold (UC) |
75	
76	> **Discipline — one system-of-record per entity.** Snowflake `Orders` is the truth; the medallion `gold` revenue is a *derived* aggregate that must reconcile **back** to it (Snowflake `order_total` ↔ Databricks `gold.daily_revenue` — the ADR 0014 `comparison` demo). Never author the same entity authoritatively in two places, or the reconciliation / FK checks measure generation noise instead of real DQ — the same guard as the key-consistency rule above.
77	>
78	> **Reserved-kind homes:** medallion **gold** tables host freshness/volume/anomaly monitors (ADR 0012 — daily aggregates that should refresh); the flat-file feeds host freshness + batch-resolution; the Snowflake↔Databricks pair hosts the cross-platform `comparison`.
79	
80	- [ ] Generator script (Python — Faker or similar) emitting CSV/Parquet to the ADLS landing zone
81	- [ ] **Keep shared keys consistent across domains AND across batch/incremental loads** — figure out a generation strategy (shared key pools / referential seeding) so the same identifier means the same entity everywhere:
82	  - `order_number` is identical across **Orders · Sales · Return · Logistics**
83	  - `sku_id` is identical across **Product · Inventory · Orders**
84	  - a shipment keeps **one `tracking_number`** across its `Ready For Shipment` → `In Transit` → `Delivered` events — and that number stays stable across different batches / data loads (events arrive incrementally)
85	  - _Why:_ without referential consistency the cross-dataset reconciliation / FK-orphan / join-based checks are meaningless (everything looks broken); the deliberate DQ issues below must be the **only** inconsistencies, not generation noise.
86	- [ ] **Shape ≥1 dataset for each reserved monitor kind** (ADR 0012 — "most real incidents are freshness/volume, not value-level; this is the leap from a GX runner to a DQ platform") so the seam has a live target when those kinds are built:
87	  - **Freshness · Volume · Anomaly** — make one feed **timestamped + incrementally loaded** (e.g. an hourly orders feed or a daily inventory snapshot with a `load_ts`): a freshness check ("last load < 1h"), a volume band ("rows within ±20%"), and an anomaly (sudden volume drop) each bite on it
88	  - **Schema drift** — one feed whose columns you can perturb across loads (add / drop / rename a column, change a type)
89	  - **Comparison / reconciliation** — the two source-vs-target pairs above (`line_total → order_total`; `order_total ↔ payment ↔ refund`) as the FDC-style diff target (ADR 0014; reserved now, built post-v1, but shape the data now)
90	  - _v1 only authors GX `expectation` checks — these kinds are **reserved** (ADR 0012); shaping the data now means the harness is ready the day they ship, no regen needed._
91	- [ ] **Seed some deliberate DQ issues** (nulls, dupes, FK orphans, stale dates, volume drops, a schema change, a reconciliation mismatch) so checks actually fail — proves severity tiers + alerting, not just green dashboards
92	- [ ] Parameterise volume + a `--seed` for reproducibility; document run command in harness README
93	
94	---
