> DEVELOPER

Tis data generation have to be in a regular credences (4 times a day, twice a day, every 2 days, etc.) how are we planning to do that?

> AGENT

Good question — and it exposes that the current generator is **one-shot** (it backfills N snapshots in a single run with wall-clock timestamps). Recurring cadence is a different mode, and there are actually *three* distinct schedules in play that are easy to conflate. Let me lay out the plan, then there's one fork that's genuinely your call.

## The three clocks (don't conflate them)

1. **Data-arrival cadence** — the *source systems* produce new data (new orders, today's inventory snapshot, the next tracking batch). **This is what you're asking about. It doesn't exist yet.**
2. **Load + trigger** — ADF/Airflow notices the new file, loads it to Snowflake/UC, and its on-success callback fires DataQ's trigger binding (§3).
3. **Check-run cadence** — DataQ runs the suite. Already shipped (Week-5 `schedules` cron table), *or* it's driven by clock #2.

Freshness/volume/anomaly only look real if **#1 keeps ticking** — data has to keep *arriving* for "last load < 1h" or "rows within ±20%" to mean anything.

## Work item that's needed regardless of mechanism: an incremental "tick" mode

The generator needs a second mode beyond the current full backfill:

- `python -m mockdata tick --feed inventory_snapshot` → emits **one** new dated batch, not all N.
- **Persisted state** (a `state.json` in the harness): the key pools (sku/customer/store ids), sequence counters (so `order_number` keeps climbing, never collides), and open shipments (so a `tracking_number` from 3 days ago gets its `delivered` event today). This is what keeps referential consistency *across ticks*, the same discipline the one-shot version already enforces *within* a run.
- Per-feed cadence is natural here: masters rarely change; orders batch a few times a day; inventory is daily; a shipment's events span days.

## The cadence engine — the fork

Cron expressions map straight onto your examples:

| Cadence | cron | Candidate feed |
|---|---|---|
| 4×/day | `0 0,6,12,18 * * *` | orders + payments increment |
| Twice/day | `0 0,12 * * *` | tracking-event batches |
| Daily | `0 1 * * *` | inventory snapshot |
| Every 2 days | `0 1 */2 * *` | feedback / reference refresh |

The question is *what reads that cron and invokes the tick*. My strong lean: **Airflow (and ADF) DAG schedules** — because the harness already stands up local Airflow in Docker (§3), Airflow is one of the two orchestration providers *under test*, and a DAG (`schedule=…` → `generate` task → `load` task → on-success triggers DataQ) exercises the exact path the harness exists to prove, with cron cadence and a UI for free. The flat-file feeds (Flow C, no load step) would be tiny generate-and-upload DAGs.

Before I build the tick mode + wire the schedules, one decision:

Flat-file (Flow C) feeds\n> become tiny `generate → upload-to-ADLS` DAGs (no load step; DataQ reads in place).\n\n- [ ] **Generator `tick` mode** (buildable now, no cloud): `python -m mockdata tick --feed <name>` emits **one** new dated batch, not the full backfill\n- [ ] **Persisted state** (`state.json` in harness): key pools (sku/customer/store ids) + sequence counters (`order_number` keeps climbing, no collisions) + open shipments (a `tracking_number` from N days ago gets its `delivered` event today) — keeps referential consistency **across ticks**, not just within one run\n- [ ] **Per-feed cadence map** (cron): masters rarely change · orders 4×/day `0 0,6,12,18 * * *` · tracking batches 2×/day `0 0,12 * * *` · inventory daily `0 1 * * *` · feedback/reference every 2 days `0 1 */2 * *`\n- [ ] **Airflow DAGs** (§3, needs local Airflow up): one scheduled DAG per feed/flow — `generate-increment` task → `load` (Snowflake/UC) or `upload` (ADLS flat-file) task → `on_success_callback` → DataQ trigger binding\n- [ ] **ADF schedule triggers** for the ADF-orchestrated subset of Flow A (parity with Airflow)\n- [ ] Verify a few cadence ticks produce **monotonic** freshness/volume series in DataQ `/results` (the monitors trend, not just one green dot)\n\n---\n\n## 3. Reference flows (each exercises a distinct DataQ run path)"
}

**Cadence engine = Airflow/ADF schedules.** Each feed gets a scheduled DAG: `generate-increment → load (Snowflake/UC) or upload (ADLS flat-file) → on-success callback → DataQ trigger binding`. Your example cadences map straight to cron:

- tracking batches **2×/day** `0 0,12 * * *`
- inventory snapshot **daily** `0 1 * * *`
- feedback/reference **every 2 days** `0 1 */2 * *`

2	
3	No secrets, no cloud calls — this module only describes *what* to generate and
4	*where each dataset lands*. The emit layer (emit.py) writes to a local out/ dir
5	mirroring the route map; uploading to ADLS / loading to Snowflake/UC is a later
6	step (§3, once Terraform §1 is applied).
7	"""

9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	from dataclasses import dataclass, field

15	# Counts are "base entities"; transactional tables fan out from them.
16	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

18	VOLUME_TIERS: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {
19	    "S": {"customers": 200, "products": 150, "stores": 8, "promos": 15, "orders": 800},
20	    "M": {

22	        "products": 600,

36	        "products": 8_000,
37	        "stores": 250,
38	        "promos": 300,
39	        "orders": 100_000,
40	    },
41	}
42	
43	DEFAULT_TIER = "S"
44	
45	
46	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
47	# Route map (§2) — which dataset lands on which DataQ run path + file format.
48	# emit.py uses `subdir` + `fmt` to lay out the output tree.
49	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
50	
51	
52	@dataclass(frozen=True)
53	class Route:
54	    """Where a generated dataset belongs in the DataQ run-path topology."""
55	
56	    flow: str  # "A-snowflake" | "A-uc" | "B-medallion" | "C-flatfile"
57	    subdir: str  # path under out/ root
58	    fmt: str  # "csv" | "parquet" | "json"
59	
60	
61	ROUTES: dict[str, Route] = {
62	    # Flow A — load to warehouse (Snowflake): transactional + financial core.
63	    "products": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/products", "csv"),
64	    "customers": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/customers", "csv"),
65	    "orders_header": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/orders_header", "csv"),
66	    "order_lines": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/order_lines", "csv"),
67	    "payments": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/payments", "csv"),
68	    "refunds": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/refunds", "csv"),
69	    "returns": Route("A-snowflake", "snowflake/returns", "csv"),
70	    # Flow A — Unity Catalog plain load: governed reference data (no medallion).
71	    "locations": Route("A-uc", "unity_catalog/locations", "parquet"),
72	    "channels": Route("A-uc", "unity_catalog/channels", "parquet"),
73	    "promotions": Route("A-uc", "unity_catalog/promotions", "parquet"),
74	    # Flow B — medallion bronze source (Databricks notebook refines bronze->silver->gold).
75	    "sales_events": Route(
76	        "B-medallion", "databricks_medallion/bronze/sales_events", "json"
77	    ),
78	    "feedback": Route("B-medallion", "databricks_medallion/bronze/feedback", "json"),
79	    # Flow C — flat-file in place (ADLS). Batch-resolved.
80	    "tracking_events": Route("C-flatfile", "adls_flatfile/logistics_tracking", "csv"),
81	    "inventory_snapshot": Route(
82	        "C-flatfile", "adls_flatfile/inventory_snapshot", "parquet"
83	    ),
84	}

89	    """Resolved generation parameters for one run."""
90	
91	    tier: str = DEFAULT_TIER
92	    seed: int = 42
93	    out_dir: str = "out"
94	    inject_issues: bool = True
95	    issue_rate: float = 0.03  # fraction of rows touched by deliberate DQ issues

98	    )

100	    counts: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)

103	        if self.tier not in VOLUME_TIERS:
104	            raise ValueError(
105	                f"unknown tier {self.tier!r}; choose from {list(VOLUME_TIERS)}"
106	            )
107	        self.counts = dict(VOLUME_TIERS[self.tier])
108	        return self

69	def _write_single(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> Path:
70	    path = target / f"{name}.{fmt}"
71	    if fmt == "csv":
72	        df.to_csv(path, index=False)
73	    elif fmt == "parquet":
74	        df.to_parquet(path, index=False)
75	    elif fmt == "json":  # NDJSON bronze raw
76	        df.to_json(path, orient="records", lines=True, date_format="iso")
77	    else:
78	        raise ValueError(f"unknown format {fmt!r}")
79	    return path
80	
81	
82	def _write_tracking(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path) -> list[Path]:
83	    paths = []
84	    for batch, group in df.groupby("batch"):
85	        path = target / f"tracking_events_{batch}.csv"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/state.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Persisted harness state for incremental `tick` generation (§2a).\n\nThe §2 backfill is stateless one-shot. Recurring ticks need continuity *across*\nruns: the same master pools, climbing id sequences (no `order_number` collisions),\nand open shipments (a tracking_number created days ago gets its next lifecycle\nevent on a later tick). That continuity lives here.\n\nLayout under <state_dir>/:\n  state.json            counters, open_shipments, last_inventory_date, batch_counter\n  masters/<name>.parquet  products, customers, locations, channels, promotions\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nMASTER_NAMES = (\"products\", \"customers\", \"locations\", \"channels\", \"promotions\")\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass HarnessState:\n    seed: int\n    tier: str\n    created_at: str\n    batch_counter: int = 0\n    counters: dict[str, int] = field(\n        default_factory=lambda: {\"order\": 0, \"line\": 0, \"return\": 0, \"feedback\": 0}\n    )\n    last_inventory_date: str | None = None\n    open_shipments: list[dict] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, d: dict) -> HarnessState:\n        return cls(**d)\n\n\ndef _state_path(state_dir: str) -> Path:\n    return Path(state_dir) / \"state.json\"\n\n\ndef _masters_dir(state_dir: str) -> Path:\n    return Path(state_dir) / \"masters\"\n\n\ndef exists(state_dir: str) -> bool:\n    return _state_path(state_dir).exists()\n\n\ndef save_state(state_dir: str, state: HarnessState) -> None:\n    Path(state_dir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    _state_path(state_dir).write_text(json.dumps(asdict(state), indent=2, default=str))\n\n\ndef load_state(state_dir: str) -> HarnessState:\n    if not exists(state_dir):\n        raise FileNotFoundError(\n            f\"no state at {state_dir!r} — run `python -m mockdata init` first\"\n        )\n    return HarnessState.from_dict(json.loads(_state_path(state_dir).read_text()))\n\n\ndef save_masters(state_dir: str, masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame]) -> None:\n    md = _masters_dir(state_dir)\n    md.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    for name in MASTER_NAMES:\n        masters[name].to_parquet(md / f\"{name}.parquet\", index=False)\n\n\ndef load_masters(state_dir: str) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:\n    md = _masters_dir(state_dir)\n    return {name: pd.read_parquet(md / f\"{name}.parquet\") for name in MASTER_NAMES}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/increment.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Incremental `tick` generators (§2a) — one batch per call, continuing state.\n\nEach feed ticks at its own cadence (driven externally by Airflow/ADF schedules):\nmasters rarely change · orders 4x/day · tracking 2x/day · inventory daily ·\nfeedback every 2 days. Referential consistency holds *across* ticks because every\ntick draws from the persisted master pools and the climbing id counters in\n`HarnessState` — the only inconsistencies are deliberate (the --issues / anomaly flags).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport datetime as dt\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .config import TAX_RATE, GenConfig\nfrom .generate import (\n    _channels,\n    _customers,\n    _locations,\n    _products,\n    _promotions,\n)\nfrom .pools import (\n    CARRIERS,\n    CHANNEL_WEIGHTS,\n    CHANNELS,\n    CITIES,\n    PAYMENT_METHODS,\n    RETURN_REASONS,\n    SURVEY_COMMENTS,\n    TRACKING_STATUSES,\n)\nfrom .rng import Rng\nfrom .state import MASTER_NAMES, HarnessState, save_masters, save_state\n\nFEEDS = (\"orders\", \"tracking\", \"inventory\", \"feedback\")\n\n# Per-tick default batch sizes (override with --count). Scales mildly with tier.\n_TICK_ORDERS = {\"S\": 40, \"M\": 120, \"L\": 400, \"XL\": 1500}\n_TICK_FEEDBACK = {\"S\": 15, \"M\": 50, \"L\": 150, \"XL\": 500}\n\n\n# --- init ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef init_state(config: GenConfig) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], HarnessState]:\n    \"\"\"Generate + persist the master pools; return them for an initial emit.\"\"\"\n    rng = Rng(config.seed)\n    now = dt.datetime.now().replace(microsecond=0)\n    c = config.counts\n    masters = {\n        \"products\": _products(rng, c[\"products\"], now),\n        \"customers\": _customers(rng, c[\"customers\"], now),\n        \"locations\": _locations(rng, c[\"stores\"]),\n        \"channels\": _channels(),\n        \"promotions\": _promotions(rng, c[\"promos\"], now),\n    }\n    state = HarnessState(\n        seed=config.seed, tier=config.tier, created_at=now.isoformat()\n    )\n    save_masters(config.state_dir, masters)\n    save_state(config.state_dir, state)\n    return masters, state\n\n\n# --- tick dispatch ---------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef tick(\n    config: GenConfig,\n    state: HarnessState,\n    masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],\n    feed: str,\n    count: int | None,\n    *,\n    inject_issues: bool = False,\n    volume_drop: bool = False,\n    stale: bool = False,\n    schema_drift: bool = False,\n) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], list[dict]]:\n    \"\"\"Produce one batch for `feed`, mutating `state` in place. Caller persists state.\"\"\"\n    state.batch_counter += 1\n    rng = Rng(config.seed + state.batch_counter)  # deterministic per (seed, tick)\n    now = dt.datetime.now().replace(microsecond=0)\n\n    if feed == \"orders\":\n        n = count or _TICK_ORDERS[config.tier]\n        return _tick_orders(rng, now, state, masters, n, inject_issues)\n    if feed == \"tracking\":\n        return _tick_tracking(rng, now, state)\n    if feed == \"inventory\":\n        return _tick_inventory(\n            rng, now, state, masters, volume_drop, stale, schema_drift\n        )\n    if feed == \"feedback\":\n        n = count or _TICK_FEEDBACK[config.tier]\n        return _tick_feedback(rng, now, state, masters, n)\n    raise ValueError(f\"unknown feed {feed!r}; choose from {FEEDS}\")\n\n\n# --- orders ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _tick_orders(\n    rng: Rng,\n    now: dt.datetime,\n    state: HarnessState,\n    masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],\n    n: int,\n    inject_issues: bool,\n) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], list[dict]]:\n    products = masters[\"products\"]\n    price_by_sku = dict(zip(products[\"sku_id\"], products[\"unit_price\"]))\n    sku_ids = products[\"sku_id\"].tolist()\n    customer_ids = masters[\"customers\"][\"customer_id\"].tolist()\n    store_ids = masters[\"locations\"][\"store_id\"].tolist()\n    promo_ids = masters[\"promotions\"][\"promo_id\"].tolist()\n\n    base_order = state.counters[\"order\"]\n    base_line = state.counters[\"line\"]\n    order_numbers = [f\"ORD-{base_order + i:08d}\" for i in range(1, n + 1)]\n\n    h: dict[str, list] = {\n        k: []\n        for k in (\n            \"order_number\", \"customer_id\", \"channel\", \"store_id\", \"promo_id\",\n            \"order_ts\", \"status\", \"subtotal\", \"tax\", \"order_total\", \"currency\",\n        )\n    }\n    lines: dict[str, list] = {\n        k: [] for k in (\"line_id\", \"order_number\", \"sku_id\", \"qty\", \"unit_price\", \"line_total\")\n    }\n    payments_rows, refunds_rows, returns_rows, sales_rows = [], [], [], []\n    line_seq = base_line\n    return_seq = state.counters[\"return\"]\n\n    for order_number in order_numbers:\n        channel = rng.weighted(CHANNELS, CHANNEL_WEIGHTS)\n        store_id = None if channel == \"online\" else rng.choice(store_ids)\n        subtotal = 0.0\n        n_lines = rng.randint(1, 5)\n        for _ in range(n_lines):\n            line_seq += 1\n            sku = rng.choice(sku_ids)\n            qty = rng.randint(1, 4)\n            unit_price = round(price_by_sku[sku], 2)\n            line_total = round(qty * unit_price, 2)\n            subtotal += line_total\n            lines[\"line_id\"].append(f\"OL-{line_seq:09d}\")\n            lines[\"order_number\"].append(order_number)\n            lines[\"sku_id\"].append(sku)\n            lines[\"qty\"].append(qty)\n            lines[\"unit_price\"].append(unit_price)\n            lines[\"line_total\"].append(line_total)\n\n        subtotal = round(subtotal, 2)\n        tax = round(subtotal * TAX_RATE, 2)\n        order_total = round(subtotal + tax, 2)\n        order_ts = now - dt.timedelta(minutes=rng.randint(0, 360))\n        status = rng.weighted(\n            [\"placed\", \"shipped\", \"delivered\", \"returned\", \"cancelled\"],\n            [0.2, 0.25, 0.4, 0.1, 0.05],\n        )\n        h[\"order_number\"].append(order_number)\n        h[\"customer_id\"].append(rng.choice(customer_ids))\n        h[\"channel\"].append(channel)\n        h[\"store_id\"].append(store_id)\n        h[\"promo_id\"].append(rng.choice(promo_ids) if rng.chance(0.3) else None)\n        h[\"order_ts\"].append(order_ts)\n        h[\"status\"].append(status)\n        h[\"subtotal\"].append(subtotal)\n        h[\"tax\"].append(tax)\n        h[\"order_total\"].append(order_total)\n        h[\"currency\"].append(\"USD\")\n\n        suffix = order_number[4:]\n        pay_status = rng.weighted([\"captured\", \"authorized\", \"failed\"], [0.9, 0.06, 0.04])\n        payments_rows.append(\n            {\n                \"payment_id\": f\"PAY-{suffix}\",\n                \"order_number\": order_number,\n                \"method\": rng.choice(PAYMENT_METHODS),\n                \"status\": pay_status,\n                \"captured_amount\": round(order_total if pay_status == \"captured\" else 0.0, 2),\n                \"paid_ts\": order_ts + dt.timedelta(minutes=rng.randint(1, 90)),\n            }\n        )\n        if status in (\"returned\", \"cancelled\"):\n            refunds_rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"refund_id\": f\"REF-{suffix}\",\n                    \"order_number\": order_number,\n                    \"refund_amount\": round(order_total * rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0), 2),\n                    \"reason\": rng.choice(RETURN_REASONS),\n                    \"refund_ts\": order_ts + dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(1, 21)),\n                }\n            )\n            return_seq += 1\n            returns_rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"return_id\": f\"RET-{return_seq:08d}\",\n                    \"order_number\": order_number,\n                    \"sku_id\": lines[\"sku_id\"][-1],\n                    \"qty\": 1,\n                    \"reason\": rng.choice(RETURN_REASONS),\n                    \"channel\": channel if channel else rng.choice(CHANNELS),\n                    \"return_ts\": now - dt.timedelta(days=rng.randint(0, 5)),\n                }\n            )\n        for k, (etype, off_h) in enumerate([(\"placed\", 0), (\"shipped\", 24), (\"audited\", 72)]):\n            sales_rows.append(\n                {\n                    \"event_id\": f\"EVT-{suffix}-{k}\",\n                    \"order_number\": order_number,\n                    \"event_type\": etype,\n                    \"event_ts\": order_ts + dt.timedelta(hours=off_h),\n                    \"channel\": channel,\n                    \"store_id\": store_id,\n                    \"amount\": order_total if etype == \"placed\" else None,\n                }\n            )\n        # Register an open shipment for orders that ship (lifecycle plays out over ticks).\n        if status in (\"shipped\", \"delivered\", \"returned\"):\n            city, _ = rng.choice(CITIES)\n            state.open_shipments.append(\n                {\n                    \"tracking_number\": f\"TRK{suffix}\",\n                    \"order_number\": order_number,\n                    \"carrier\": rng.choice(CARRIERS),\n                    \"city\": city,\n                    \"stage\": 0,\n                }\n            )\n\n    state.counters[\"order\"] = base_order + n\n    state.counters[\"line\"] = line_seq\n    state.counters[\"return\"] = return_seq\n\n    datasets = {\n        \"orders_header\": pd.DataFrame(h),\n        \"order_lines\": pd.DataFrame(lines),\n        \"payments\": pd.DataFrame(payments_rows),\n        \"refunds\": pd.DataFrame(refunds_rows),\n        \"returns\": pd.DataFrame(returns_rows),\n        \"sales_events\": pd.DataFrame(sales_rows),\n    }\n    issues = _inject_order_issues(datasets, rng) if inject_issues else []\n    return datasets, issues\n\n\ndef _inject_order_issues(datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], rng: Rng) -> list[dict]:\n    issues: list[dict] = []\n    oh, ol = datasets[\"orders_header\"], datasets[\"order_lines\"]\n    if len(oh) >= 3:\n        idx = oh.sample(n=max(1, len(oh) // 20), random_state=1).index\n        oh.loc[idx, \"channel\"] = None\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"null\", \"dataset\": \"orders_header\", \"detail\": \"channel NULL\", \"n\": len(idx), \"keys\": oh.loc[idx, \"order_number\"].tolist()})\n        idx2 = oh.sample(n=max(1, len(oh) // 30), random_state=2).index\n        oh.loc[idx2, \"order_total\"] = (oh.loc[idx2, \"order_total\"] * 1.15).round(2)\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"reconciliation\", \"dataset\": \"orders_header\", \"detail\": \"order_total inflated\", \"n\": len(idx2), \"keys\": oh.loc[idx2, \"order_number\"].tolist()})\n    if len(ol) >= 3:\n        idx3 = ol.sample(n=max(1, len(ol) // 30), random_state=3).index\n        ol.loc[idx3, \"sku_id\"] = \"SKU-000000\"\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"fk_orphan\", \"dataset\": \"order_lines\", \"detail\": \"sku_id -> SKU-000000\", \"n\": len(idx3), \"keys\": ol.loc[idx3, \"line_id\"].tolist()})\n    return issues\n\n\n# --- tracking (advance open shipments one lifecycle stage) ------------------\n\n\ndef _tick_tracking(\n    rng: Rng, now: dt.datetime, state: HarnessState\n) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], list[dict]]:\n    rows, still_open = [], []\n    for ship in state.open_shipments:\n        stage = ship[\"stage\"]\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"tracking_number\": ship[\"tracking_number\"],\n                \"order_number\": ship[\"order_number\"],\n                \"carrier\": ship[\"carrier\"],\n                \"status\": TRACKING_STATUSES[stage],\n                \"event_ts\": now - dt.timedelta(minutes=rng.randint(0, 120)),\n                \"location_city\": ship[\"city\"],\n            }\n        )\n        ship[\"stage\"] = stage + 1\n        if ship[\"stage\"] < len(TRACKING_STATUSES):\n            still_open.append(ship)  # more events to come on later ticks\n    state.open_shipments = still_open\n    return {\"tracking_events\": pd.DataFrame(rows)}, []\n\n\n# --- inventory (one daily snapshot over the stable grid) --------------------\n\n\ndef _tick_inventory(\n    rng: Rng,\n    now: dt.datetime,\n    state: HarnessState,\n    masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],\n    volume_drop: bool,\n    stale: bool,\n    schema_drift: bool,\n) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], list[dict]]:\n    sku_ids = masters[\"products\"][\"sku_id\"].tolist()\n    store_ids = masters[\"locations\"][\"store_id\"].tolist()\n    grid_skus = sku_ids[:400]\n    grid_stores = store_ids[:20]\n    load_ts = now - (dt.timedelta(hours=26) if stale else dt.timedelta(0))\n    snap_date = now.date()\n\n    rows = [\n        {\n            \"sku_id\": sku,\n            \"store_id\": store,\n            \"on_hand\": rng.randint(0, 500),\n            \"planned\": rng.randint(0, 200),\n            \"snapshot_date\": snap_date,\n            \"load_ts\": load_ts,\n        }\n        for sku in grid_skus\n        for store in grid_stores\n    ]\n    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n    issues: list[dict] = []\n    if volume_drop:\n        df = df.sample(frac=0.3, random_state=9).reset_index(drop=True)\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"volume_drop\", \"dataset\": \"inventory_snapshot\", \"detail\": f\"{snap_date} thinned to ~30%\", \"n\": 1, \"keys\": [str(snap_date)]})\n    if stale:\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"stale\", \"dataset\": \"inventory_snapshot\", \"detail\": f\"{snap_date} load_ts +26h back\", \"n\": 1, \"keys\": [str(snap_date)]})\n    if schema_drift:\n        df = df.rename(columns={\"on_hand\": \"qty_on_hand\"})\n        issues.append({\"kind\": \"schema_drift\", \"dataset\": \"inventory_snapshot\", \"detail\": f\"{snap_date} on_hand -> qty_on_hand\", \"n\": 1, \"keys\": [str(snap_date)]})\n\n    state.last_inventory_date = str(snap_date)\n    return {\"inventory_snapshot\": df}, issues\n\n\n# --- feedback --------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _tick_feedback(\n    rng: Rng, now: dt.datetime, state: HarnessState, masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], n: int\n) -> tuple[dict[str, pd.DataFrame], list[dict]]:\n    customers = masters[\"customers\"]\n    sample = customers.sample(n=min(n, len(customers)), random_state=state.batch_counter)\n    base_fb = state.counters[\"feedback\"]\n    rows = []\n    for i, cust in enumerate(sample.itertuples(index=False), start=1):\n        rows.append(\n            {\n                \"feedback_id\": f\"FB-{base_fb + i:08d}\",\n                \"customer_id\": cust.customer_id,\n                \"channel\": rng.weighted(CHANNELS, CHANNEL_WEIGHTS),\n                \"rating\": rng.randint(1, 5),\n                \"comment\": rng.choice(SURVEY_COMMENTS),\n                \"email\": cust.email,\n                \"created_ts\": now,\n            }\n        )\n    state.counters[\"feedback\"] = base_fb + len(rows)\n    return {\"feedback\": pd.DataFrame(rows)}, []\n"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/emit.py",
  "old_string": "def _write_single(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> Path:\n    path = target / f\"{name}.{fmt}\"\n    if fmt == \"csv\":\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"parquet\":\n        df.to_parquet(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"json\":  # NDJSON bronze raw\n        df.to_json(path, orient=\"records\", lines=True, date_format=\"iso\")\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown format {fmt!r}\")\n    return path",
  "new_string": "def _write_single(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> Path:\n    path = target / f\"{name}.{fmt}\"\n    _write_df(df, path, fmt)\n    return path\n\n\ndef _write_df(df: pd.DataFrame, path: Path, fmt: str) -> None:\n    if fmt == \"csv\":\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"parquet\":\n        df.to_parquet(path, index=False)\n    elif fmt == \"json\":  # NDJSON bronze raw\n        df.to_json(path, orient=\"records\", lines=True, date_format=\"iso\")\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown format {fmt!r}\")\n\n\ndef write_batch(\n    datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],\n    config: GenConfig,\n    batch_id: str,\n    feed: str,\n    issues: list[dict],\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Write one incremental tick (§2a): batch-suffixed files into the route subdirs.\n\n    ADLS landing zones are append-style (a new file per load), so each tick lands a\n    distinct `<name>_<batch_id>.<fmt>` rather than overwriting. Appends one line to\n    out/_ticks.jsonl and (per issue) out/_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl.\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(config.out_dir)\n    entry: dict = {\"batch_id\": batch_id, \"feed\": feed, \"datasets\": {}}\n    for name, df in datasets.items():\n        route = ROUTES[name]\n        target = root / route.subdir\n        target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        path = target / f\"{name}_{batch_id}.{route.fmt}\"\n        _write_df(df, path, route.fmt)\n        entry[\"datasets\"][name] = {\"rows\": int(len(df)), \"file\": str(path.relative_to(root))}\n\n    with (root / \"_ticks.jsonl\").open(\"a\") as f:\n        f.write(json.dumps(entry, default=str) + \"\\n\")\n    if issues:\n        with (root / \"_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl\").open(\"a\") as f:\n            for it in issues:\n                f.write(json.dumps({**it, \"batch_id\": batch_id}, default=str) + \"\\n\")\n    return entry"
}

1	"""CLI: python -m mockdata --tier S --seed 42 --out ./out
2	
3	Reproducible per (--tier, --seed) except wall-clock-anchored timestamps (so the
4	latest inventory snapshot / tracking-delivered batch are genuinely fresh — see
5	generate.py). Use --no-issues to emit clean data (all checks green).
6	"""
7	
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import argparse
11	
12	from .config import DEFAULT_TIER, VOLUME_TIERS, GenConfig
13	from .dq_issues import inject
14	from .emit import write
15	from .generate import generate
16	
17	
18	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
19	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mockdata", description=__doc__)
20	    parser.add_argument("--tier", choices=list(VOLUME_TIERS), default=DEFAULT_TIER)
21	    parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42)
22	    parser.add_argument("--out", default="out", help="output directory root")
23	    parser.add_argument(
24	        "--snapshots", type=int, default=5, help="inventory daily snapshots"
25	    )
26	    parser.add_argument("--issue-rate", type=float, default=0.03)
27	    parser.add_argument(
28	        "--no-issues",
29	        dest="inject_issues",
30	        action="store_false",
31	        help="emit clean data (no deliberate DQ issues)",
32	    )
33	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
34	
35	    config = GenConfig(
36	        tier=args.tier,
37	        seed=args.seed,
38	        out_dir=args.out,
39	        inject_issues=args.inject_issues,
40	        issue_rate=args.issue_rate,
41	        snapshots=args.snapshots,
42	    ).resolve()
43	
44	    print(f"generating tier={config.tier} seed={config.seed} -> {config.out_dir}/")
45	    datasets = generate(config)
46	    issues = inject(datasets, config) if config.inject_issues else []
47	    manifest = write(datasets, config, issues)
48	
49	    total_rows = sum(m["rows"] for m in manifest.values())
50	    print(f"  {len(manifest)} datasets, {total_rows:,} rows")
51	    for name, m in manifest.items():
52	        print(
53	            f"    {name:<20} {m['rows']:>8,} rows  [{m['flow']}]  {len(m['files'])} file(s)"
54	        )
55	    if issues:
56	        print(
57	            f"  {len(issues)} deliberate DQ issues -> {config.out_dir}/_dq_issues_manifest.json"
58	        )
59	        for it in issues:
60	            print(
61	                f"    {it['kind']:<14} {it['dataset']:<20} n={it['n']:<5} {it['detail']}"
62	            )
63	    return 0
64	
65	
66	if __name__ == "__main__":
67	    raise SystemExit(main())
68

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/__main__.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CLI for the DataQ harness mock-data generator.\n\nThree subcommands:\n  backfill   one-shot full generation (§2) — masters + N snapshots + deliberate issues\n  init       create persisted state + emit the master/reference loads once (§2a)\n  tick       emit ONE incremental batch for a feed (§2a) — driven on a cadence by\n             Airflow/ADF schedules (orders 4x/day, tracking 2x/day, inventory daily, ...)\n\nReproducible per (--tier, --seed) except wall-clock-anchored timestamps (so the\nlatest snapshots/batches are genuinely fresh — see generate.py).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\n\nfrom .config import DEFAULT_TIER, VOLUME_TIERS, GenConfig\nfrom .dq_issues import inject\nfrom .emit import write, write_batch\nfrom .generate import generate\nfrom .increment import FEEDS, init_state, tick\nfrom .state import load_masters, load_state, save_state\n\n\ndef _common(parser: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:\n    parser.add_argument(\"--tier\", choices=list(VOLUME_TIERS), default=DEFAULT_TIER)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--seed\", type=int, default=42)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--out\", default=\"out\", help=\"output directory root\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--state\", default=\"state\", help=\"state directory (§2a)\")\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"mockdata\", description=__doc__)\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"cmd\", required=True)\n\n    p_back = sub.add_parser(\"backfill\", help=\"one-shot full generation (§2)\")\n    _common(p_back)\n    p_back.add_argument(\"--snapshots\", type=int, default=5)\n    p_back.add_argument(\"--issue-rate\", type=float, default=0.03)\n    p_back.add_argument(\n        \"--no-issues\", dest=\"inject_issues\", action=\"store_false\",\n        help=\"emit clean data (no deliberate DQ issues)\",\n    )\n\n    p_init = sub.add_parser(\"init\", help=\"create state + emit reference loads (§2a)\")\n    _common(p_init)\n\n    p_tick = sub.add_parser(\"tick\", help=\"emit one incremental batch for a feed (§2a)\")\n    _common(p_tick)\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--feed\", required=True, choices=list(FEEDS))\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--count\", type=int, default=None, help=\"batch size (orders/feedback)\")\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--issues\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"inject row-level DQ issues\")\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--volume-drop\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"(inventory) thin snapshot ~30%\")\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--stale\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"(inventory) push load_ts +26h back\")\n    p_tick.add_argument(\"--schema-drift\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"(inventory) rename on_hand\")\n\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    config = GenConfig(\n        tier=args.tier, seed=args.seed, out_dir=args.out, state_dir=args.state\n    ).resolve()\n\n    if args.cmd == \"backfill\":\n        return _run_backfill(config, args)\n    if args.cmd == \"init\":\n        return _run_init(config)\n    return _run_tick(config, args)\n\n\ndef _run_backfill(config: GenConfig, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:\n    config.inject_issues = args.inject_issues\n    config.issue_rate = args.issue_rate\n    config.snapshots = args.snapshots\n    print(f\"backfill tier={config.tier} seed={config.seed} -> {config.out_dir}/\")\n    datasets = generate(config)\n    issues = inject(datasets, config) if config.inject_issues else []\n    manifest = write(datasets, config, issues)\n    total = sum(m[\"rows\"] for m in manifest.values())\n    print(f\"  {len(manifest)} datasets, {total:,} rows\")\n    for name, m in manifest.items():\n        print(f\"    {name:<20} {m['rows']:>8,} rows  [{m['flow']}]  {len(m['files'])} file(s)\")\n    if issues:\n        print(f\"  {len(issues)} deliberate DQ issues -> {config.out_dir}/_dq_issues_manifest.json\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef _run_init(config: GenConfig) -> int:\n    print(f\"init state={config.state_dir}/ tier={config.tier} seed={config.seed}\")\n    masters, _state = init_state(config)\n    # Emit the reference/master loads once (Flow A Snowflake + UC reference data).\n    manifest = write(masters, config, [])\n    for name, m in manifest.items():\n        print(f\"    {name:<20} {m['rows']:>6,} rows  [{m['flow']}]\")\n    print(f\"  state initialised -> {config.state_dir}/state.json (+ masters/)\")\n    print(\"  now drive ticks on a cadence, e.g.:\")\n    print(\"    python -m mockdata tick --feed orders     # 4x/day\")\n    print(\"    python -m mockdata tick --feed inventory  # daily\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef _run_tick(config: GenConfig, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:\n    state = load_state(config.state_dir)\n    masters = load_masters(config.state_dir)\n    datasets, issues = tick(\n        config, state, masters, args.feed, args.count,\n        inject_issues=args.issues,\n        volume_drop=args.volume_drop, stale=args.stale, schema_drift=args.schema_drift,\n    )\n    batch_id = f\"b{state.batch_counter:06d}\"\n    entry = write_batch(datasets, config, batch_id, args.feed, issues)\n    save_state(config.state_dir, state)\n\n    rows = sum(d[\"rows\"] for d in entry[\"datasets\"].values())\n    print(f\"tick #{state.batch_counter} feed={args.feed} batch={batch_id}: {rows:,} rows\")\n    for name, d in entry[\"datasets\"].items():\n        print(f\"    {name:<20} {d['rows']:>6,} rows -> {d['file']}\")\n    if args.feed in (\"orders\", \"tracking\"):\n        print(f\"    open shipments carried forward: {len(state.open_shipments)}\")\n    if issues:\n        for it in issues:\n            print(f\"    issue: {it['kind']:<14} {it['dataset']:<20} n={it['n']} {it['detail']}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}

> AGENT

Done. Tick mode + state persistence are built, verified, and documented.

## What shipped (§2a — recurring cadence prerequisite)

Three new modules in `mockdata/`, CLI restructured into subcommands:

- **`state.py`** — persists master pools (`state/masters/*.parquet`) + `state/state.json` (id counters, open-shipment registry, batch counter).
- **`increment.py`** — per-feed incremental generators: `orders` (header+lines+payments+refunds+returns+sales_events), `tracking` (advances open shipments one lifecycle stage), `inventory` (one daily snapshot, with `--volume-drop`/`--stale`/`--schema-drift` flags), `feedback`.
- **`emit.write_batch`** — append-style batch-suffixed files (`<name>_b000123.<fmt>`) + `_ticks.jsonl` / `_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl`.
- **CLI** — `backfill` (the old §2 one-shot) · `init` (seed state + emit reference loads once) · `tick --feed <name>`.

## Verified guarantees (the hard part — continuity *across* ticks)

| Property | Result |
|---|---|
| `order_number` across ticks | 1–40 then 41–80, **0 collisions** |
| `tracking_number` across the 3 lifecycle batches | identical set; status advances `ready_for_shipment → in_transit → delivered` |
| Cross-feed FK integrity | all tracking orders exist in orders_header ✓ |
| State drains correctly | open_shipments → 0 after delivery; counters climb |
| ruff + black | clean |

The key win: a `tracking_number` created on an orders tick now gets its successive lifecycle events on *later* tracking ticks — the across-*ticks* version of the §2 requirement, which is exactly what makes freshness/volume monitors trend over time.

**What's left for the cadence (all §3, needs cloud):** the Airflow DAGs / ADF schedule triggers that *call* these ticks on the cron cadences (`tick → load/upload → on-success triggers DataQ`). Those are gated on the Terraform apply.

Next logical step is still the **§1 apply decision** (register the two RPs + greenlight), which unblocks §3 — including wiring these ticks to Airflow. Want me to scaffold the §3 Airflow DAGs now (authorable against the tick CLI even before apply), or hold?

> DEVELOPER

Hey, for the regular file creation can we utilize a function app, or will it be over kill?

> AGENT

Good instinct to question it — and "overkill" isn't quite the right axis. Cost-wise a timer-triggered Function is basically free at this cadence, so it's not overkill there. The real issues are **shape fit** and **one hidden cost**.

**It doesn't conflict with the §2a decision — it refines it.** A Function App here plays a *different role* than the cadence decision covered: it's the **source-system simulator** (drops raw files into the ADLS landing zone), which is genuinely *upstream* of the orchestrator. Airflow/ADF stay the things-under-test that watch the landing zone → load → trigger DataQ. So "Function produces files, ADF/Airflow react" is arguably *more* realistic than having the orchestrator both produce and orchestrate. No contradiction.

**But for this specific workload, a Function App is slightly the wrong shape:**

| | Function App (timer) | Container Apps **Job** (cron) | Tick inside Airflow DAG |
|---|---|---|---|
| Heavy deps (pandas/pyarrow) | chunky package, slow cold start (latency irrelevant here, but friction) | **your existing image just runs** | just runs |
| Scheduler | NCRONTAB timer ✓ | cron schedule ✓ | DAG `schedule` ✓ |
| Platform reuse | new resource type | **same Container Apps as DataQ** | reuses Airflow that's up for §3 |
| Producer/orchestrator separation | clean | clean | muddied (orchestrator produces) |
| New infra to operate | a Function App | a Job def | none |

Functions shine for *lightweight, event-driven glue*. A pandas/pyarrow **batch** job is more naturally a **Container Apps Job** — and you're already on Container Apps for DataQ, so your deps and image carry over with zero packaging quirks, with cron built in.

**The one real cost either serverless route forces:** the generator's state (`state.json` + `state/masters/*.parquet`) currently lives on local disk. Functions and Jobs are **ephemeral**, so state must move to **blob storage** — a focused change to `state.py` (swap the filesystem load/save for a blob backend, pointing at the same ADLS account). That's the actual work, and it's the same whether you pick Functions or a Job.

So: not overkill on cost, but Functions are a shape mismatch here; if you want a managed Azure scheduler for file creation, a Container Apps Job fits better. The decision changes what I'd scaffold next (and adds the blob-state refactor):

> AGENT

Recorded. Here's where that leaves us — a clean two-layer design:

- **File creation** = Container Apps **Job** (cron) running the `mockdata` image → drops files into the ADLS landing zone. *Upstream* source-system simulator.
- **Load + trigger** = ADF/Airflow watch the landing zone → load → trigger DataQ (the path under test). Flow C skips the load (DataQ reads in place).

The choice adds three work items (now in §2a), two of which are **buildable/testable now without the apply**:

1. **`state.py` pluggable backend** — abstract persistence behind a `StateBackend` seam: `LocalStateBackend` (today) + `AdlsStateBackend` (blob), since the Job is ephemeral. The seam + local impl are fully testable now; the ADLS impl is codeable now, runnable against real ADLS only post-apply.
2. **`Dockerfile`** for the `mockdata` image — `docker build` + `docker run` testable locally right now.
3. **Container Apps Job Terraform** (`azurerm_container_app_job` + `schedule_trigger`) — authorable now but apply-gated (needs a Container Apps Environment + registry).

Want me to build #1 (state backend seam + ADLS impl) and #2 (Dockerfile) now? Both are self-contained and I can verify the seam + container locally; only the live ADLS/Job wiring waits on the Terraform apply.

For Load + trigger = ADF/Airflow  -  don't keep all the jobs based on file land, keep some based on schedule time also

Good call — that's how real orchestration estates actually look: some pipelines fire on file-landing (event), others on a clock (scheduled batch window). Mixing both also makes the harness exercise *both* ADF/Airflow trigger modalities and DataQ's 10-min polling / gap-recovery fallback (which only really bites on scheduled pipelines). Let me capture it.

> AGENT

Captured. The load+trigger layer is now explicitly a **mix**, not all event-driven:

- **Event / file-landing** (ADF Storage-Event trigger · Airflow file-sensor/dataset DAG) → high-frequency feeds: **orders, tracking**
- **Schedule / time** (ADF Schedule or tumbling-window · Airflow cron) → daily/batch feeds: **inventory, feedback** — with the load schedule offset to fire *after* the file-creation Job (e.g. Job drops inventory 01:00 → scheduled load 01:15, so there's something to pick up)

That gives one event + one schedule pipeline per warehouse target (Snowflake & UC) for parity, and it's what makes DataQ's pipeline-run monitoring + 10-min polling/gap-recovery actually get exercised (the poll only meaningfully matters on scheduled pipelines). The per-feed split is noted as adjustable.

This is all §3 (post-apply) wiring — recorded, nothing to build yet on this layer.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/state.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Persisted harness state for incremental `tick` generation (§2a).\n\nThe §2 backfill is stateless one-shot. Recurring ticks need continuity *across*\nruns: the same master pools, climbing id sequences (no `order_number` collisions),\nand open shipments (a tracking_number created on an earlier tick gets its next\nlifecycle event on a later tick). That continuity lives here.\n\nStorage is behind a **`StateBackend` seam** so the same state works whether ticks\nrun locally (filesystem) or as the ephemeral **Container Apps Job** (ADLS blob —\nlocal disk doesn't survive between Job runs). Pick the backend by URI:\n  local path            -> LocalStateBackend   (e.g. ./state)\n  adls://<acct>/<fs>/<p> -> AdlsStateBackend    (e.g. adls://dataqhX/landing/state)\n\nLogical contents (relative names the backend stores):\n  state.json              counters, open_shipments, last_inventory_date, batch_counter\n  masters/<name>.parquet  products, customers, locations, channels, promotions\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom abc import ABC, abstractmethod\nfrom dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field\nfrom io import BytesIO\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nMASTER_NAMES = (\"products\", \"customers\", \"locations\", \"channels\", \"promotions\")\nSTATE_FILE = \"state.json\"\n\n\n# --- state record ----------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass HarnessState:\n    seed: int\n    tier: str\n    created_at: str\n    batch_counter: int = 0\n    counters: dict[str, int] = field(\n        default_factory=lambda: {\"order\": 0, \"line\": 0, \"return\": 0, \"feedback\": 0}\n    )\n    last_inventory_date: str | None = None\n    open_shipments: list[dict] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, d: dict) -> HarnessState:\n        return cls(**d)\n\n\n# --- backend seam ----------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass StateBackend(ABC):\n    \"\"\"Byte storage for state.json + masters/*.parquet. Local FS or ADLS blob.\"\"\"\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool: ...\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes: ...\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None: ...\n\n\nclass LocalStateBackend(StateBackend):\n    def __init__(self, root: str) -> None:\n        self.root = Path(root)\n\n    def _p(self, rel: str) -> Path:\n        return self.root / rel\n\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool:\n        return self._p(rel).exists()\n\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes:\n        return self._p(rel).read_bytes()\n\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None:\n        p = self._p(rel)\n        p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        p.write_bytes(data)\n\n\nclass AdlsStateBackend(StateBackend):\n    \"\"\"ADLS Gen2 blob backend for the ephemeral Container Apps Job.\n\n    Auth via DefaultAzureCredential — managed identity in the Job, `az login` (or\n    AZURE_* env SP) locally. azure-* libs are imported lazily so the package runs\n    with the local backend even when they aren't installed.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, account: str, filesystem: str, prefix: str) -> None:\n        from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential\n        from azure.storage.filedatalake import DataLakeServiceClient\n\n        self.prefix = prefix.strip(\"/\")\n        svc = DataLakeServiceClient(\n            account_url=f\"https://{account}.dfs.core.windows.net\",\n            credential=DefaultAzureCredential(),\n        )\n        self._fs = svc.get_file_system_client(filesystem)\n\n    def _path(self, rel: str) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.prefix}/{rel}\" if self.prefix else rel\n\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool:\n        return self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).exists()\n\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes:\n        return self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).download_file().readall()\n\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None:\n        self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).upload_data(data, overwrite=True)\n\n\ndef make_backend(uri: str) -> StateBackend:\n    \"\"\"`adls://<account>/<filesystem>/<prefix...>` -> ADLS; anything else -> local path.\"\"\"\n    if uri.startswith(\"adls://\"):\n        rest = uri[len(\"adls://\") :]\n        parts = rest.split(\"/\", 2)\n        if len(parts) < 2:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"bad adls uri {uri!r}; expected adls://<account>/<filesystem>[/<prefix>]\"\n            )\n        account, filesystem = parts[0], parts[1]\n        prefix = parts[2] if len(parts) == 3 else \"\"\n        return AdlsStateBackend(account, filesystem, prefix)\n    return LocalStateBackend(uri)\n\n\n# --- state + masters IO (backend-agnostic) ---------------------------------\n\n\ndef state_exists(backend: StateBackend) -> bool:\n    return backend.exists(STATE_FILE)\n\n\ndef save_state(backend: StateBackend, state: HarnessState) -> None:\n    backend.write_bytes(\n        STATE_FILE, json.dumps(asdict(state), indent=2, default=str).encode()\n    )\n\n\ndef load_state(backend: StateBackend) -> HarnessState:\n    if not state_exists(backend):\n        raise FileNotFoundError(\"no state found — run `python -m mockdata init` first\")\n    return HarnessState.from_dict(json.loads(backend.read_bytes(STATE_FILE)))\n\n\ndef save_masters(backend: StateBackend, masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame]) -> None:\n    for name in MASTER_NAMES:\n        buf = BytesIO()\n        masters[name].to_parquet(buf, index=False)\n        backend.write_bytes(f\"masters/{name}.parquet\", buf.getvalue())\n\n\ndef load_masters(backend: StateBackend) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:\n    return {\n        name: pd.read_parquet(BytesIO(backend.read_bytes(f\"masters/{name}.parquet\")))\n        for name in MASTER_NAMES\n    }\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Both built and verified.

## §2a state backend + Dockerfile — done

**`state.py` pluggable backend**
- `StateBackend` seam → `LocalStateBackend` (default) + `AdlsStateBackend` (Gen2 blob via `DefaultAzureCredential`; azure libs lazy-imported so local runs need neither).
- `make_backend()` picks by URI: `adls://<acct>/<fs>/<prefix>` → ADLS, anything else → local path.
- Verified the local tick flow is intact after the refactor (counters climb, ruff+black clean). The ADLS impl is coded; runnable against real ADLS only post-apply.

**Docker image** (`Dockerfile` + `.dockerignore` at harness root)
- `python:3.13-slim` + pandas/pyarrow/azure, `ENTRYPOINT python -m mockdata`; `.dockerignore` keeps `secrets.sh`/terraform/out/state out of the build context.
- Verified end-to-end: image builds, in-container `init`+`tick` write the route layout, **state survives across separate container runs**, `azure SDK OK` in the image.

## One honest gap this surfaced (flagged in the tracker, not silently dropped)

I built the **state** backend, but the **output** writer (`emit.py`'s `--out`) still targets local FS / a mount. In my container smoke test that worked because I mounted a volume — but the real ephemeral Job has **no mount**, so for it to drop files into the landing zone, `--out adls://…` needs the *same* ADLS routing the state backend now has. That's the **remaining piece before the Job is fully standalone** — a sibling change to `emit.py`, buildable now (no cloud needed to write the code; only live ADLS to fully test).

It's small and symmetric with what I just did. Want me to do the **emit→ADLS output backend** now so the image is a complete, self-contained Job — or pause here and pick it up when we tackle the Container Apps Job Terraform (which is apply-gated anyway)?

```
1	"""Write generated datasets to a local out/ tree mirroring the §2 route map.
2	
3	Layout (out/ root):
4	  snowflake/<table>/<table>.csv                 Flow A -> Snowflake (COPY INTO source)
5	  unity_catalog/<table>/<table>.parquet         Flow A -> UC plain load
6	  databricks_medallion/bronze/<src>/<src>.json  Flow B -> medallion bronze (NDJSON)
7	  adls_flatfile/logistics_tracking/tracking_events_<batch>.csv   Flow C (event-append)
8	  adls_flatfile/inventory_snapshot/inventory_<date>.parquet      Flow C (daily snapshot)
9	
10	Two split datasets become multiple files:
11	  tracking_events  -> one CSV per `batch`        (same tracking_number recurs across files)
12	  inventory_snapshot -> one Parquet per `snapshot_date`
13	
14	Schema-drift (the one cross-file deliberate issue) is applied here: the newest
15	inventory snapshot file renames on_hand -> qty_on_hand, appended to the DQ manifest.
16	
17	Writes two sidecar manifests at the out/ root:
18	  _manifest.json            dataset -> {flow, format, files, rows}
19	  _dq_issues_manifest.json  the deliberate failures (row-level from dq_issues + schema drift)
20	"""
21	
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import json
25	from pathlib import Path
26	
27	import pandas as pd
28	
29	from .config import ROUTES, GenConfig
30	
31	
32	def write(
33	    datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], config: GenConfig, dq_issues: list[dict]
34	) -> dict:
35	    root = Path(config.out_dir)
36	    root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
37	    file_manifest: dict[str, dict] = {}
38	
39	    for name, df in datasets.items():
40	        route = ROUTES[name]
41	        target = root / route.subdir
42	        target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
43	
44	        if name == "tracking_events":
45	            files = _write_tracking(df, target)
46	        elif name == "inventory_snapshot":
47	            files, drift = _write_inventory(df, target, config.inject_issues)
48	            if drift:
49	                dq_issues.append(drift)
50	        else:
51	            files = [_write_single(df, target, name, route.fmt)]
52	
53	        file_manifest[name] = {
54	            "flow": route.flow,
55	            "format": route.fmt,
56	            "rows": len(df),
57	            "files": [str(p.relative_to(root)) for p in files],
58	        }
59	
60	    (root / "_manifest.json").write_text(
61	        json.dumps(file_manifest, indent=2, default=str)
62	    )
63	    (root / "_dq_issues_manifest.json").write_text(
64	        json.dumps(dq_issues, indent=2, default=str)
65	    )
66	    return file_manifest
67	
68	
69	def _write_single(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> Path:
70	    path = target / f"{name}.{fmt}"
71	    _write_df(df, path, fmt)
72	    return path
73	
74	
75	def _write_df(df: pd.DataFrame, path: Path, fmt: str) -> None:
76	    if fmt == "csv":
77	        df.to_csv(path, index=False)
78	    elif fmt == "parquet":
79	        df.to_parquet(path, index=False)
80	    elif fmt == "json":  # NDJSON bronze raw
81	        df.to_json(path, orient="records", lines=True, date_format="iso")
82	    else:
83	        raise ValueError(f"unknown format {fmt!r}")
84	
85	
86	def write_batch(
87	    datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
88	    config: GenConfig,
89	    batch_id: str,
90	    feed: str,
91	    issues: list[dict],
92	) -> dict:
93	    """Write one incremental tick (§2a): batch-suffixed files into the route subdirs.
94	
95	    ADLS landing zones are append-style (a new file per load), so each tick lands a
96	    distinct `<name>_<batch_id>.<fmt>` rather than overwriting. Appends one line to
97	    out/_ticks.jsonl and (per issue) out/_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl.
98	    """
99	    root = Path(config.out_dir)
100	    entry: dict = {"batch_id": batch_id, "feed": feed, "datasets": {}}
101	    for name, df in datasets.items():
102	        route = ROUTES[name]
103	        target = root / route.subdir
104	        target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
105	        path = target / f"{name}_{batch_id}.{route.fmt}"
106	        _write_df(df, path, route.fmt)
107	        entry["datasets"][name] = {
108	            "rows": len(df),
109	            "file": str(path.relative_to(root)),
110	        }
111	
112	    with (root / "_ticks.jsonl").open("a") as f:
113	        f.write(json.dumps(entry, default=str) + "\n")
114	    if issues:
115	        with (root / "_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl").open("a") as f:
116	            for it in issues:
117	                f.write(json.dumps({**it, "batch_id": batch_id}, default=str) + "\n")
118	    return entry
119	
120	
121	def _write_tracking(df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path) -> list[Path]:
122	    paths = []
123	    for batch, group in df.groupby("batch"):
124	        path = target / f"tracking_events_{batch}.csv"
125	        group.drop(columns=["batch"]).to_csv(path, index=False)
126	        paths.append(path)
127	    return paths
128	
129	
130	def _write_inventory(
131	    df: pd.DataFrame, target: Path, inject_drift: bool
132	) -> tuple[list[Path], dict | None]:
133	    paths = []
134	    drift: dict | None = None
135	    latest_date = df["snapshot_date"].max()
136	    for snap_date, group in df.groupby("snapshot_date"):
137	        out = group.drop(columns=["snapshot_date"])
138	        if inject_drift and snap_date == latest_date:
139	            out = out.rename(
140	                columns={"on_hand": "qty_on_hand"}
141	            )  # schema drift on newest load
142	            drift = {
143	                "kind": "schema_drift",
144	                "dataset": "inventory_snapshot",
145	                "detail": f"newest snapshot {snap_date} renames on_hand -> qty_on_hand",
146	                "n": 1,
147	                "keys": [str(snap_date)],
148	            }
149	        path = target / f"inventory_{snap_date}.parquet"
150	        out.to_parquet(path, index=False)
151	        paths.append(path)
152	    return paths, drift
153	
```

This is the natural sibling of the state backend — and rather than duplicate the ADLS auth in two places, I'll factor the storage primitive into a shared `storage.py` that both `state.py` and `emit.py` use. Let me create it:

> TOOL

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/storage.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Byte storage seam shared by state (§2a) and emit output.\n\nOne abstraction, two homes: the persisted tick state and the generated landing-zone\nfiles both need \"write bytes to a relative path\", on either local disk (dev / mounted\nvolume) or ADLS Gen2 blob (the ephemeral Container Apps Job, which has no durable disk).\n\n  local path             -> LocalStore   (e.g. ./out, ./state)\n  adls://<acct>/<fs>/<p>  -> AdlsStore    (e.g. adls://dataqhX/landing, .../landing/state)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom abc import ABC, abstractmethod\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n\nclass Store(ABC):\n    \"\"\"Relative-path byte storage. `append_text` powers the jsonl manifests.\"\"\"\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool: ...\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes: ...\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None: ...\n\n    def append_text(self, rel: str, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Append (read-modify-write by default; LocalStore overrides with a real append).\"\"\"\n        existing = self.read_bytes(rel) if self.exists(rel) else b\"\"\n        self.write_bytes(rel, existing + text.encode())\n\n\nclass LocalStore(Store):\n    def __init__(self, root: str) -> None:\n        self.root = Path(root)\n\n    def _p(self, rel: str) -> Path:\n        return self.root / rel\n\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool:\n        return self._p(rel).exists()\n\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes:\n        return self._p(rel).read_bytes()\n\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None:\n        p = self._p(rel)\n        p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        p.write_bytes(data)\n\n    def append_text(self, rel: str, text: str) -> None:\n        p = self._p(rel)\n        p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        with p.open(\"a\") as f:\n            f.write(text)\n\n\nclass AdlsStore(Store):\n    \"\"\"ADLS Gen2 store for the Container Apps Job.\n\n    Auth via DefaultAzureCredential — managed identity in the Job, `az login` (or\n    AZURE_* env SP) locally. azure-* libs are imported lazily so the package runs\n    with the local store even when they aren't installed.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, account: str, filesystem: str, prefix: str) -> None:\n        from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential\n        from azure.storage.filedatalake import DataLakeServiceClient\n\n        self.prefix = prefix.strip(\"/\")\n        svc = DataLakeServiceClient(\n            account_url=f\"https://{account}.dfs.core.windows.net\",\n            credential=DefaultAzureCredential(),\n        )\n        self._fs = svc.get_file_system_client(filesystem)\n\n    def _path(self, rel: str) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.prefix}/{rel}\" if self.prefix else rel\n\n    def exists(self, rel: str) -> bool:\n        return self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).exists()\n\n    def read_bytes(self, rel: str) -> bytes:\n        return self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).download_file().readall()\n\n    def write_bytes(self, rel: str, data: bytes) -> None:\n        self._fs.get_file_client(self._path(rel)).upload_data(data, overwrite=True)\n\n\ndef make_store(uri: str) -> Store:\n    \"\"\"`adls://<account>/<filesystem>/<prefix...>` -> ADLS; anything else -> local path.\"\"\"\n    if uri.startswith(\"adls://\"):\n        parts = uri[len(\"adls://\") :].split(\"/\", 2)\n        if len(parts) < 2:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"bad adls uri {uri!r}; expected adls://<account>/<filesystem>[/<prefix>]\"\n            )\n        account, filesystem = parts[0], parts[1]\n        prefix = parts[2] if len(parts) == 3 else \"\"\n        return AdlsStore(account, filesystem, prefix)\n    return LocalStore(uri)\n"
}

overwrite=True)\n\n\ndef make_backend(uri: str) -> StateBackend:\n    \"\"\"`adls://<account>/<filesystem>/<prefix...>` -> ADLS; anything else -> local path.\"\"\"\n    if uri.startswith(\"adls://\"):\n        rest = uri[len(\"adls://\") :]\n        parts = rest.split(\"/\", 2)\n        if len(parts) < 2:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"bad adls uri {uri!r}; expected adls://<account>/<filesystem>[/<prefix>]\"\n            )\n        account, filesystem = parts[0], parts[1]\n        prefix = parts[2] if len(parts) == 3 else \"\"\n        return AdlsStateBackend(account, filesystem, prefix)\n    return LocalStateBackend(uri)\n\n\n# --- state + masters IO (backend-agnostic) ---------------------------------\n\n\ndef state_exists(backend: StateBackend) -> bool:\n    return backend.exists(STATE_FILE)\n\n\ndef save_state(backend: StateBackend, state: HarnessState) -> None:\n    backend.write_bytes(\n        STATE_FILE, buf.getvalue())\n\n\ndef load_masters(backend: StateBackend) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:\n    return {\n        name: pd.read_parquet(BytesIO(backend.read_bytes(f\"masters/{name}.parquet\")))\n        for name in MASTER_NAMES\n    }",

asdict, dataclass, field\nfrom io import BytesIO\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .storage import Store\n\nMASTER_NAMES = (\"products\", \"customers\", \"locations\", \"channels\", \"promotions\")\nSTATE_FILE = \"state.json\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass HarnessState:\n    seed: int\n    tier: str\n    created_at: str\n    batch_counter: int = 0\n    counters: dict[str, int] = -----------------------------------\n\n\ndef state_exists(store: Store) -> bool:\n    return store.exists(STATE_FILE)\n\n\ndef save_state(store: Store, state: HarnessState) -> None:\n    store.write_bytes(\n        STATE_FILE, json.dumps(asdict(state), indent=2, default=str).encode()\n    )\n\n\ndef load_state(store: Store) -> HarnessState:\n    if not state_exists(store):\n        raise FileNotFoundError(\"no state found — run `python -m mockdata init` first\")\n    return HarnessState.from_dict(json.loads(store.read_bytes(STATE_FILE)))\n\n\ndef save_masters(store: Store, masters: dict[str, pd.DataFrame]) -> None:\n    for name in MASTER_NAMES:\n        buf = BytesIO()\n        masters[name].to_parquet(buf, index=False)\n        store.write_bytes(f\"masters/{name}.parquet\", buf.getvalue())\n\n\ndef load_masters(store: Store) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:\n    return {\n        name: pd.read_parquet(BytesIO(store.read_bytes(f\"masters/{name}.parquet\")))\n        for name in MASTER_NAMES\n    }"

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/mockdata/emit.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Write generated datasets to the §2 route-map tree, on any `Store` (storage.py) —\nlocal FS for dev / a mounted volume, or ADLS Gen2 blob for the Container Apps Job\n(`--out adls://<acct>/<filesystem>[/<prefix>]`). Paths are relative; the store roots them.\n\nLayout under the out root:\n  snowflake/<table>/<table>.csv                 Flow A -> Snowflake (COPY INTO source)\n  unity_catalog/<table>/<table>.parquet         Flow A -> UC plain load\n  databricks_medallion/bronze/<src>/<src>.json  Flow B -> medallion bronze (NDJSON)\n  adls_flatfile/logistics_tracking/tracking_events_<batch>.csv   Flow C (event-append)\n  adls_flatfile/inventory_snapshot/inventory_<date>.parquet      Flow C (daily snapshot)\n\n`write` = one-shot backfill (§2). `write_batch` = one incremental tick (§2a),\nbatch-suffixed (`<name>_<batch_id>.<fmt>`) since landing zones are append-style.\n\nSidecar manifests at the out root:\n  _manifest.json / _ticks.jsonl            what was written\n  _dq_issues_manifest.json[l]              the deliberate failures\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom io import BytesIO\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom .config import ROUTES, GenConfig\nfrom .storage import Store, make_store\n\n\ndef _df_bytes(df: pd.DataFrame, fmt: str) -> bytes:\n    if fmt == \"csv\":\n        return df.to_csv(index=False).encode()\n    if fmt == \"parquet\":\n        buf = BytesIO()\n        df.to_parquet(buf, index=False)\n        return buf.getvalue()\n    if fmt == \"json\":  # NDJSON bronze raw\n        return df.to_json(orient=\"records\", lines=True, date_format=\"iso\").encode()\n    raise ValueError(f\"unknown format {fmt!r}\")\n\n\ndef _put(store: Store, rel: str, df: pd.DataFrame, fmt: str) -> str:\n    store.write_bytes(rel, _df_bytes(df, fmt))\n    return rel\n\n\ndef write(\n    datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame], config: GenConfig, dq_issues: list[dict]\n) -> dict:\n    store = make_store(config.out_dir)\n    file_manifest: dict[str, dict] = {}\n\n    for name, df in datasets.items():\n        route = ROUTES[name]\n        if name == \"tracking_events\":\n            files = _write_tracking(store, df)\n        elif name == \"inventory_snapshot\":\n            files, drift = _write_inventory(store, df, config.inject_issues)\n            if drift:\n                dq_issues.append(drift)\n        else:\n            files = [_put(store, f\"{route.subdir}/{name}.{route.fmt}\", df, route.fmt)]\n\n        file_manifest[name] = {\n            \"flow\": route.flow,\n            \"format\": route.fmt,\n            \"rows\": int(len(df)),\n            \"files\": files,\n        }\n\n    store.write_bytes(\"_manifest.json\", json.dumps(file_manifest, indent=2, default=str).encode())\n    store.write_bytes(\"_dq_issues_manifest.json\", json.dumps(dq_issues, indent=2, default=str).encode())\n    return file_manifest\n\n\ndef write_batch(\n    datasets: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],\n    config: GenConfig,\n    batch_id: str,\n    feed: str,\n    issues: list[dict],\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"One incremental tick (§2a): batch-suffixed files into the route subdirs.\"\"\"\n    store = make_store(config.out_dir)\n    entry: dict = {\"batch_id\": batch_id, \"feed\": feed, \"datasets\": {}}\n    for name, df in datasets.items():\n        route = ROUTES[name]\n        rel = f\"{route.subdir}/{name}_{batch_id}.{route.fmt}\"\n        _put(store, rel, df, route.fmt)\n        entry[\"datasets\"][name] = {\"rows\": int(len(df)), \"file\": rel}\n\n    store.append_text(\"_ticks.jsonl\", json.dumps(entry, default=str) + \"\\n\")\n    for it in issues:\n        store.append_text(\n            \"_dq_issues_manifest.jsonl\", json.dumps({**it, \"batch_id\": batch_id}, default=str) + \"\\n\"\n        )\n    return entry\n\n\ndef _write_tracking(store: Store, df: pd.DataFrame) -> list[str]:\n    subdir = ROUTES[\"tracking_events\"].subdir\n    files = []\n    for batch, group in df.groupby(\"batch\"):\n        rel = f\"{subdir}/tracking_events_{batch}.csv\"\n        files.append(_put(store, rel, group.drop(columns=[\"batch\"]), \"csv\"))\n    return files\n\n\ndef _write_inventory(\n    store: Store, df: pd.DataFrame, inject_drift: bool\n) -> tuple[list[str], dict | None]:\n    subdir = ROUTES[\"inventory_snapshot\"].subdir\n    files: list[str] = []\n    drift: dict | None = None\n    latest_date = df[\"snapshot_date\"].max()\n    for snap_date, group in df.groupby(\"snapshot_date\"):\n        out = group.drop(columns=[\"snapshot_date\"])\n        if inject_drift and snap_date == latest_date:\n            out = out.rename(columns={\"on_hand\": \"qty_on_hand\"})  # schema drift on newest load\n            drift = {\n                \"kind\": \"schema_drift\",\n                \"dataset\": \"inventory_snapshot\",\n                \"detail\": f\"newest snapshot {snap_date} renames on_hand -> qty_on_hand\",\n                \"n\": 1,\n                \"keys\": [str(snap_date)],\n            }\n        files.append(_put(store, f\"{subdir}/inventory_{snap_date}.parquet\", out, \"parquet\"))\n    return files, drift\n"
}

> AGENT

Done. The `mockdata` image is now a fully self-contained Job.

## emit→ADLS output backend — done

Rather than duplicate the ADLS auth, I **factored the byte-storage primitive into a shared `storage.py`**:

- **`Store` seam** → `LocalStore` + `AdlsStore` + `make_store(uri)` — one ADLS-auth impl, now used by **both** `state.py` and `emit.py` (the old `state.py`-local backend classes were removed; it imports from `storage`).
- **`emit.py`** routes every write through the store: DataFrames → bytes via buffers (csv/parquet/json), manifests via `write_bytes` / `append_text` (jsonl). `--out adls://<acct>/<fs>[/<prefix>]` now writes the landing zone directly; a local path still writes the filesystem.

## Verified

- Local **backfill + init + tick** intact through the new store; ruff + black clean.
- In the rebuilt image: `make_store` routing correct (`local→LocalStore`, `adls://→AdlsStore`), prefix parsed, lazy azure import resolves, DataLake client constructs, and a write **reaches the Azure auth layer** — failing only with `ClientAuthenticationError` (no identity present). In the Job, managed identity supplies the token.

So both halves — state *and* output — now share one ADLS path, and a Job invocation is fully standalone (no mount):

```bash
python -m mockdata tick --feed inventory \
  --out adls://dataqharnessXXXX/landing --state adls://dataqharnessXXXX/landing/state
```

Tracker + README + memory updated. The only thing left for this layer is the **Container Apps Job Terraform** (`azurerm_container_app_job` + `schedule_trigger` + a registry for the image) — authorable now but apply-gated, so it slots in with §3.

That clears every **buildable-now** §2a item. The remaining harness work (Container Apps Job TF, the ADF/Airflow load+trigger wiring, §3 flows) is all apply-gated — so the **§1 `terraform apply`** (register the two RPs + greenlight) is now the single thing unblocking everything downstream. > AGENT

Per the recorded plan: **local Docker on the dev box** — [HARNESS_TODO.md:30](HARNESS_TODO.md#L30) says *"Airflow is local Docker (§3); Terraform does NOT provision Airflow… ADF is used in its managed cloud form."* So the split is deliberate: **ADF = managed Azure** (the cloud-orchestrator reference), **Airflow = local docker-compose** (the second `OrchestrationProvider` impl, stood up disposably in §3).

That still works even though file-gen and the datasources are now in the cloud, because Airflow only needs to be a **network client**: a local scheduler can load cloud Snowflake/UC, read the ADLS landing zone, and POST the HMAC callback to DataQ's `/orchestration/events/airflow` webhook. DataQ doesn't care where Airflow runs — only that it gets the callback or can be polled.

**But two honest wrinkles surface from our recent cloud decisions** (worth being explicit since we just said "event/file-landing trigger for orders+tracking via Airflow"):

1. **No true push events to a laptop.** ADF gets native Event-Grid storage-event triggers; a *local* Airflow behind NAT can't receive an Event-Grid push, so its "file-landing" feeds use a **poll-based file sensor** (DAG waits on the file) instead of a push. Semantically still file-landing-triggered — just poll, not push. Fine for the harness, and it actually exercises DataQ's polling path nicely.
2. **The box must be running** for schedules to fire / sensors to poll. Acceptable for a demo; not "unattended in the cloud."

If you'd rather have Airflow always-on and receiving real events, the natural cloud upgrade — since we already provision an ADF factory — is **ADF Managed Airflow** (Azure runs Airflow as a feature of Data Factory; keeps both orchestrators under one roof). Alternatives: Airflow on Container Apps (reuse the platform) or a small always-on VM. All cost compute, vs. local Docker being free/disposable.

My recommendation: **keep local Docker for the harness** — it's sufficient to prove DataQ's Airflow integration (callback + polling fallback), matches the plan, and stays disposable. Treat ADF Managed Airflow as the opt-in upgrade only if you want the always-on / real-push behavior later.

Want me to leave it as local Docker, or switch the plan to ADF Managed Airflow (or one of the other cloud hosts)?
